**Document one, the circular — the words.** *Model Endorsements for Continuity Filings (Flood Seasons).* Series **MC/FS**, sealed by the **Standing Office of Provincial Continuities**. Base issue of the **second month of this year**, run **five hundred and sixty**. Amendment sheet of the **twenty-second of the ninth month**, run **nine hundred and forty**, carrying the Mosswake public-necessity sentence **verbatim** as the third line of a form of words, under a note permitting a court to adopt that wording or any other: *Adopted from a provincial court, the ninth month. A court may adopt this wording or any other.* Distribution: all district courts and landing offices holding a flood docket, filed with the standing instructions and not with the papers.

**Document two, the First Seat quarterly return — the counting.** *Continuity Filings, Flood Seasons, Categorisation.* Sealed with the old fire-and-water device of the First Seat, no office name. **It is a permission, not a crime, and its correct use is administrative.** It says nothing about validity, and paragraph four says so expressly.

**No document connects them.** There is no instrument anywhere in the empire saying the First Seat issued the form because the office printed the circular, and the mechanism is a practice across two offices in two buildings four hundred miles apart. **The only two people who know both documents exist are Marek Kest and Tamsin Rook.** Tamsin's live objection to leaving the quarterly return out of the reply of the thirteenth is in **her own book**, on a page about the Millbrook gauge, not in Marek's coat.

**The circular was named in the Cinder Court referral and was not produced and not examined there.** The registrar put three questions in open court — whether the nine paragraphs, the roll-keeper's four lines, and the MC/FS series were before the court — and Marek Kest answered no to all three, and the registrar wrote the answers down. The Cinder Court therefore holds no evidence about the document that carries its own second order's wording, and Volume 02 must not treat the court as having ruled on the circular. The referral's last of its eleven things it does not ask for is that no court be asked to find a person.

### 2. The Cinder Court's four orders, and exactly what each binds

Made on the **seventeenth of the sixth month**, in **room four**, **Sela Vint presiding**, the registrar unnamed and about fifty.

1. **No guarantor duty attaches to a person who has not been heard in a room and has not signed, and no court in this empire may join a person as guarantor on the strength of a printed model of general wording.** It **binds that court and any court that takes a recognition from it. It does not reach back to a district decision already made in a district that has not been heard by that court** — and the Mosswake Roll Court's own decision of the twenty-first of the fifth month **stands**, because it was made in a room.
2. **The sentence stated by the Mosswake Roll Court on the twelfth of the ninth month, as printed in the amendment sheet of the twenty-second, is recorded by the Cinder Court as model wording of general application and is not a decision of any court.** It **may not be the recorded reason for a decision in any district without a hearing of the person it names.** The record of the sentence is a record of a document and is **not** a record of a law, is not a precedent, and no district is permitted to use it as a reason. **It does not withdraw the sentence anywhere, because only the district that stated it can withdraw its own practice, and that district has not withdrawn it and has not been asked to.** It is still in nine hundred and forty printed copies on the ordinary distribution to forty-six addresses.
3. **The seal certificates of Corvin Dray are suspended — sixty-one of them** — on the **registrar's own authority**, with no argument heard. With the order go the count of the certificates, the sale of a third witness mark in ninety-eight instances of which twenty-two are in the Reed Cut district, six in the Venn file and one in the Dunn file, and **a mark** — a filled square with a bar through it and a notch cut out of the top left corner — against **thirty-eight lines in three districts**. **The mark is entered in a public minute book as a mark and not as a name, and no chapter may convert it into a named house or a named client.** The court does not know what house keeps it.
4. **The sealed instruction standing on the Venn file is escalated by the Cinder Court, on its own motion, to a full sitting with jurisdiction, to be heard when the court's list permits.** It was on the court's own motion **over the applicant's objection**, which Marek Kest raised and lost, and which is entered in the minute: that the escalation places the only question in the referral in a list for a year, giving it a date and no name. **There is no date.**

### 3. The entry by device, and its operative line

A court with four hundred years of practice has assumed a document has a person at the top of it. Where one does not, the Cinder Court enters the device. The form was drawn in the reign of the third of the Ashfall line for a case nobody can now find, is used about twice in a decade, and the registrar read the whole of it aloud in seventeen minutes.

**Operative line, in substance: where a document is produced whose face bears the seal of a seat and no name, the seat is entered as a respondent by its device; no person of or belonging to that seat is party, cited or examinable; and no inference of any kind may be drawn in that proceeding from the fact that a person has not been named.** The entry is made in the open minute book with the device struck in wax in the presence of the clerk at the back of the room.

The registrar's own gloss, which is canon: the line has been read aloud in a hearing **four times in nine years, and three of those were read by a person who was relieved.** It does not say a person was not named; it says the court will not draw a conclusion about a person out of the shape of a document. **It is the only rule in the compact's own procedure that does any work on a record of nothing but names.** Nineteen of them were in the room's file; six of the people were in the room; **none of them was at the top of anything.**

**A court that declines to name a person is not thereby wrong**, and Volume 02 may not treat the entry by device as a trick, an evasion, or a defect to be got round.

### 4. The sixty households of the sixth year

**About sixty households in the Mosswake district**, to whom the Mosswake Roll Court applied the public-necessity sentence **in letters and without a hearing** between the third month of the sixth year and the sixth year. The court entered that **it does not know their names, cannot get them in time, and that they are not before it, are not represented, and have not asked to be.** It also entered that it **will not order the district to do anything in place of the sentence, cannot, and that the district will have to work out in the sixth month what it does in a flood when it cannot use it** — a cost named so that nobody can say afterwards it was not known.

**Nobody in Volume 01 has gone looking for them.** This is the volume's largest unpaid debt and the search for them is Volume 02 work. Holder: the Mosswake Roll Court, by its own minute of the twenty-first of the fifth month. Ivet Sarn is the clerk of the window where any of them might come.

### 5. The pattern of five offices, and the sentence that matters

Found by the registrar of the Cinder Court, out of a document, in a minute, and entered as a **registrar's note and not evidence**: five bodies, each in the name of a person or a body that **did exactly what it was required to do.**

1. A **district water office** that issued a general memorandum on the eighth of the eighth month, naming no parcel, and received a particular correction under it the next day, and did not read it.
2. A **provincial court** that received that correction, entered it, and did not strike it.
3. A **Chancery standing office** that issued, in a numbered series, a sheet on the twenty-second of the ninth month — ten days after a sentence was stated in a court in another province — carrying that sentence as approved model wording, to forty-six addresses.
4. A **landing office** three hundred miles down the water that stamped a creditor's filled-in copy of the printed model into the routine column in about four seconds, and cannot now produce a document, and told the truth about the stamp when she was asked in front of two witnesses.
5. A **dyers' combination** in a furnace town with a rule thirty-one years old at the second leaf of its own book, by which a house in the trade may not write to a court, a guild, a factor's office, a district office or a stranger about an instrument without the assessor's leave in writing first.

**The sentence that matters, in the registrar's own words: no two of these five are within four hundred miles of each other. Four of them can no longer do harm. The third can do it again tomorrow, and there is nobody in it, and it is not a person.**

**A list of offices is a route and it is not an answer.** It is worse than what Marek asked for and it is the only thing there is. Holder: the registrar's note in the minute of the seventeenth of the sixth month, in room four.

### 6. The custody chain, unchanged

None of the following moved in Volume 01 and none of it may be moved by implication.

- **The Venn source leaf** is in the locked cabinet of the **Notaries' Table at Auremar**, on the north wall of the third gallery, key on a chain round whichever supervisor is on duty. **It did not move in this volume.** It came to Mosswake only because Ivet Sarn sent her own rider on the district court's custody slip on the night of the tenth of the ninth month: it came down the river on the eleventh, was held sealed in her strong box from that afternoon, and was produced in open court at ten on the twelfth out of a Table sleeve with the Table's seal on the wax, on the magistrate's own direction. A comparison cannot be had by a clerk who is not standing in the room when the sleeve comes out; a district court has no power to open that cabinet; **Sarn has no Table key and never had one.** A written application, a court direction, and a recorded custody entry are all three required.
- **The provincial hold** on the Venn file is sealed, not to be enforced, removed or assigned, **and its reference carries no office name.** The fire-and-water device of the First Seat is on the seal.
- **The First Seat's device was first named aloud in open court on the twelfth of the ninth month**, by Magistrate Rell. It has not been given a name on the face of anything since.
- **The second sealed page is shut.** There is **one refusal against Marek Kest's name in a form in Examinations** and **the second recorded decision not to open it**, which is item six of the handover list in his own hand, signed on the twelfth of the eighth month. **There is no third refusal, and none may be staged.** Anyone who opens it must be witnessed, must pay for looking, and must not find a person in it.
- **The counting-house mark is a mark and is not a name.** It is in the public minute book of the Cinder Court as a mark, against thirty-eight lines in three districts, and the court does not know what house keeps it.
- **The red stitch, the thread in the form's fold, and the working stitch in the Ashfall bundle are three different things, and no two of them may be joined in a sentence.** Marek's prohibition, entered on the **eighteenth of the ninth month of last year**, stands and does not expire because a third red fact has been found. The Ashfall bundle is on the second shelf of the **Received Room**: *Ashfall branch, fourth shelf, uncollated. Received the ninth month of the year twelve years back. Damaged, four items, uncollated.* **Not opened.** Eleven first clerks of the year have looked at it and gone and done something else, and one of them is Marek Kest.
- **The Kest property-and-debt record is formally reopened and is not cleared.** Any person at the counter of the third gallery may buy a shilling's copy of the property, the debt, and the four openings and closings since the fire. **Ilyra Kest's name remains a redaction in the public roll of the year of the fire and a blank in a Chancery roll in Greyfen, in a tin in the house of a locksmith, who is not to be written to by anybody in the building without his saying so first.** Reopening is the opposite of clearing.

### 7. The calendar, so that Volume 02 does not re-derive it

**The volume runs from the seventh of the ninth month of the ninth year to the twenty-second of the eighth month of the year after it**, in five batches of ten.

- Batch 0001: the seventh to the eighteenth of the ninth month, all ten chapters inside one month.
- Batch 0002: the nineteenth of the ninth month to the ninth of the eleventh.
- Batch 0003: the eleventh of the eleventh to the twenty-second of the second month of the year after.
- Batch 0004: the second week of the second month to the tenth of the fourth.
- Batch 0005: the third week of the fifth month to the twenty-second of the eighth.

**All five day-by-day calendars are already in this file** — one under each batch heading in the chronology section, with every fixed date and every card reading that had to be interpreted. **Volume 02 does not re-derive any of them.** The Batch 0005 calendar is the one that matters for the hand-over, and it is the last calendar in the file.

**Weekday names are not usable as anchors.** Exactly one is on the page — the ninth of the fourth month is a Monday, because Chapter 38 has the sealed scope decision arriving on Monday morning — and the lengths of the fourth, fifth, sixth and seventh months are nowhere fixed, so **Volume 02 uses no weekday name at all.** Chapters 41–50 use no real month name either; months are numbered on the page throughout the closing batch. Date the day and let the reader do the arithmetic.

**Four repair passes are recorded**: one on Batch 0003, two on Batch 0004, one on Batch 0005. Three of the four are summarised at the head of this file and all four are set out in the per-batch notes in `state/batch-summary.md`. Batches 0001 and 0002 have had none. Every move those passes made is listed at the head of this file, and every divergence between a card and the page is recorded there as a card reading and not a canon change.

### 8. The two rules most likely to be broken by accident

- **The two documents must not be merged.** The MC/FS circular is a Chancery standing office's paper; the First Seat quarterly return is a dragon seat's paper. They are not connected by any document, one is not the child of the other, and the circular was not before the Cinder Court and is not examined there. **Only Marek Kest and Tamsin Rook know both exist.** A Volume 02 chapter that puts them in one sentence, one file, one reply, or one argument has broken the volume's spine.
- **The four lines and the sentence have different ages and must never be merged.** The four substituted lines are in the Sallow file in the **fourth month of the fifth year** and are in twenty-two of thirty-one MC/FS sheets in the rack. The public-necessity sentence was **stated on the twelfth of the ninth month and printed on the twenty-second.** No chapter may put the sentence in an older file, and no chapter may put the four lines in a newer form than the one already traced. **Chapter 44 turned the Caudle notice's resemblance to the fourth term into a fact about a number and a date, and it is a fact about a number and a date; it must not be turned back into a seam.**

# VOLUME 02

**Volume 02 is *The First Hearing*, Chapters 51–100, in five batches of ten. Batches 0001 and 0002 are written: Chapters 51–70. `outline/volume-02.md` is the volume outline. `outline/batches/volume-02-batch-0001.md` and `outline/batches/volume-02-batch-0002.md` are the card sources for the two batches written, each with a closing section recording its divergences between cards and page.** The next chapter range is **71–80** and there is no Volume 02 chapter outside 51–70 yet.

| 51 | 3,401 | 56 | 3,389 |
