### The Corrections Made On The Page Before Volume 03 Batch 0005 Closed, And Checked Clean

**Twelve calendar month names and six weekday names were written into the ten chapters and every one of them was found and corrected before the batch closed.** A check of all ten files for both now returns nothing but the modal verb *may* and the ordinary use of *I have*. The record, so that the next writer does not repeat them:

1. **Chapter 0141** — *nine weeks of July* → *nine weeks of the seventh month*; *a lane in July* → *a lane in the seventh month*; *in June* → *in the sixth month*.
2. **Chapter 0143** — *came to a yard in May* → *came to a yard in the fifth month*; *I am not starting in the seventh month of my life* → **I am not starting at sixty-four**, which was also an ambiguity, a *seventh month* being a calendar word.
3. **Chapter 0144** — *a wet week in June* twice → *in the sixth month* twice; *changes his mind on a Friday* → *changes his mind in a city in the wet and tells nobody*; *the weekly comes on a Friday* → *the weekly comes once a week whether there is anything in it or not*; *did not come on the Friday* → **did not come on the next weekly either, and the one after that, and the volume closed with it still not come**, which is also the truer fact.
4. **Chapter 0145** — *in June* → *in the sixth month*.
5. **Chapter 0147** — *since May* → *since the fifth month*; *on a Friday* → *in a city and tells nobody*.
6. **Chapter 0148** — *in the second week of July* twice → *in the second week of the seventh month* twice.
7. **Chapter 0149** — *since May* → *since the fifth month*.
8. **Chapter 0150** — *in June* → *in the sixth month of last year*; *in September* three times → *in the ninth month of last year* three times.

**Three defects of repetition were found by a paragraph-and-sentence audit across Chapters 140–150 and all three were corrected before the batch closed.** (a) Chapter 0143 opened with a byte-identical repeat of Chapter 0140's biographical paragraph for Doreth Ferrow; it was rewritten in the same facts and the same voice without the identical wording. (b) Chapter 0141 quoted Rennick Adley's own entry of the thirtieth of the sixth month verbatim from Chapter 0140; rather than alter a book record that exists in the manuscript, the chapter now gives the entry in the book-keeper's own narration in four sentences, with the fourth quoted, and says that it is four sentences long and the last of them is the reason it is there. (c) Chapters 0142 and 0148 both stated the watch in the same sentence; Chapter 0148 now restates it as two sentences in its own words. **A re-audit of all ten chapters returns zero duplicate paragraphs and zero duplicate sentences.**

### The Rules Established On The Page In Volume 03 Batch 0005

1. **A rotation is not a list. It is one question asked sixty times, in sixty doorways, by a person who can be asked why, so that sixty answers exist and every one of them is one person's and nobody's else.** The question has to be the same question every time and it has to be one a person can say no to, and **the sixth line — a person who says no is not asked again — is what makes it one.** (Chapters 0141, 0143.)
2. **A duty that stops is not the same as a duty that survives stopping.** The difference is a name entered before the term ends, and a name cannot be entered before a person has been asked, and asking is about nine weeks of doorways, and about nineteen of about sixty will say no, **and the nineteen are still behind that bank.** (Chapter 0147.)
3. **A term with a review is a duty with a door in it, and the door has to be walked through by a person on a day, and there is no office in this empire whose door that is, so the door is a person — and if the person does not come the duty stops and the water does not stop.** (Chapters 0142 and 0147.)
4. **A person who holds a thing stops being asked, and the duty does not stop.** Said by a woman of about thirty-one at her own door, in about nine sentences, in the first week of the seventh month, and it is the finding the whole batch is built on. (Chapter 0141.)
5. **A count is a thing you can act on and a yes is not**, and a chair who has spent nineteen years collecting yeses is going to hand on a number. **And a moving number in a book stops being a record and becomes an opinion**, and a foreman may refuse to have one written down and give her reason. (Chapters 0143 and 0147.)
6. **A season on that bank costs the Company nothing at all, and the whole of the cost is the wages. A wage is the price and the price is the point, and everything else in this matter for four months has been free.** (Chapter 0141, and priced out loud in 0142.)
7. **An exception is a piece of paper. A speech can be made about people who are not in the room and a paper cannot, and a paper with a name at the foot of it requires that somebody has been asked — not spoken to, not written down, asked.** And **that is the only law in this matter that has never once been broken in fourteen months, by a House, a guild, a court, a dragon or a foreman, and it is not a law and its author does not believe it is anybody's doing.** (Chapter 0148.)
8. **A person can write a sheet that stops its own author.** The line that forbade entering a person on a schedule who had not been asked was written by Marek Kest himself in the second week of the seventh month, and it is the instrument that forbade him in the fourth week of the ninth, and **the first thing in fourteen months that stopped him doing anything was not a court, not a House and not Tamsin Rook.** (Chapter 0148.)
9. **A person who is not paid is doing a favour, and a favour has no end and no terms and no notice, and you cannot tell a favour to stop.** And **that is the reason a reader cannot be paid, and it is not the money.** (Chapter 0149.)
10. **A reader who is paid can be told to stop, and that is the first arrangement in this matter in which the person who turns up can be told to stop, and it is not a wall.** (Chapters 0149 and 0150.)
11. **A person who is thanked is a person who reads for thanks**, and a thanks has to be withdrawn in a book, in a request, with a reason entered, and the reason is that in about four years somebody is going to ask for it for nothing and say it is her mother's river. (Chapters 0149 and 0150.)
12. **About nine of the sixty cannot read a paragraph, so a rotation of about forty-one people of whom nine cannot read a paragraph is not a duty, it is a notice — and a person who reads and is not in the rotation is a person who cannot be told to stop.** The reader is therefore on the schedule at the same rate as every other person. (Chapter 0147.)
13. **A list of works with a term in it has an end in it and a list of persons does not. A person on a list of works is on it for a season and comes off, and the day they come off is a public day, and a person on a list of persons is on it until a man with a tray changes his mind in a city and tells nobody.** Eleven words, and it is the whole of what a duty is. (Chapter 0144.)
14. **A column with a heading over it is a machine for turning a house's problem into a family's problem, and a column with no heading over it is the same book with the machine taken out** — and **the same protection has now arrived in two trades about four hundred and thirty miles apart, in about four months, independently, and neither of the two women who found it can tell you why it works.** (Chapter 0145, against Chapter 0112 and Chapter 0146.)
15. **A refusal in a form is not a declination to answer**, and a declination would leave it open for somebody to ask the same question in four years in a different room and get a different answer. (Chapter 0146.)
16. **A column with a date in it and a person against the date is a hearing, and a hearing is the only thing in this empire that makes anybody answerable to anything, and a court of limited jurisdiction cannot make one**, and a person who has not been heard has not been given a duty. And **if this court had a seventh column, about nine hundred and forty people would be summoned about four at a time to rooms about four hundred miles from where they live, and the court would be doing to them the exact thing the district of this court did to them in the sixth year, with a better motive and a worse timetable.** (Chapter 0146.)
17. **A border law that does not need a person answers faster than a House's own table, and the reason is that one only had to be written down and the other had to be certified.** And **that is the second book in two months in which a House has had to put something about itself in writing.** (Chapter 0144.)
18. **A described person is a person and is harder to serve and easier to lose, and one of about forty-one people on a rotation is entered as a description and not by name, at her own insistence, twice**, and a rotation can be made out of described persons and the making of one does not reduce the count. (Chapters 0141, 0147 and 0150.)
19. **A person can be found by almost nothing at all, and the paper is not what finds them; the paper only makes it possible to find them afterwards.** And **every other form in this matter has been drafted in the direction of making it possible to say yes without asking, and not one of them has succeeded.** (Chapter 0148.)
20. **A person who is a foreman and has never once been on a piece of paper finds that the price of being on one is that about forty people can now find out where she is on any given day**, and that it is a page and not a habit. (Chapter 0147.)

### The Third Exception, As Established On The Page In Chapter 0150

**This is Stage 2, Burden-Bearer, and it is bound by `outline/volume-03.md` bound obligation one and by `bible/power-system.md`, and the volume has now paid it on the page.** It is four lines on the back of a sheet in a clerk's hand, **unsealed, with no number and no series and no heading and a date**, and it is a record and not a magic, and it will be produced in about four years by anybody who wants it, and there is no way in this empire to give a name back.

- **The consequence specified:** the flood duty of the bank at the third-of-the-way mark, and of about one hundred and forty feet of ditch on the south side, and of the scour at that place. **All three were on nobody's list and are on this one from the second of the fifth month of next year.**
- **Moved from:** a named party, being Marek Kest, who was the person holding it by the withdrawal of his own first instrument.
- **Moved to:** the persons named on the schedule in rotation, one season each, at forty-five pence a day, the same rate as every other person on it, with a day stated against every name.
- **The rights in it:** a right to give it up on one day's notice, in the person's own hand, into a book that has no heading; a review at the end of each term on a date stated against that name; the schedule to be read to any person on it and to any person in any house behind the bank by a person entered on the schedule for that purpose and at the same rate as every other person on it.
- **The limits in it, and they are the Stage 2 limit and it is honoured:** no person is entered who has not been asked in that person's own doorway, and no person is entered who cannot say no, and the answer and the day are entered. **About forty-one have been asked and about nineteen have said no and about four have not been asked and never will be, and all three of those facts are on the last page of the volume.**
- **The cost named:** his own labour for the length of the term and his own liability for it, **and there is no other cost, and nothing in it is a debt of any household, and no child is named in it or is party to it.**
- **What it is not:** not a seal, not a certification, not a precedent, not a magic, not an order, not a discharge of anybody, and not a thing that decides anything on its own.
- **The mark:** a symptom of a legal burden. It reads nothing, decides nothing, imposes no geographic tether and no obligation that is not written on the paper, and **it does not know where the ford is.** It flared once, in the fourth hour, for about nine seconds, and **told nobody anything.** **The mark is not the price. The wage is the price.**
- **The word:** *exception*. It is used of three instruments in Volume 03 and nothing else — the first exception of the twelfth of the fourth month, the second exception of the first week of the fifth, and this one — and the words *exception* and *precedent* are not used of the Venn certification, which has five operative terms and is never said to have four.

### The Withdrawal Of The First Exception, And What It Cost

**Dated the twenty-fourth of the ninth month of last year, effective the second of the fifth month of next year, read out in nine sentences in a bay in front of about thirty people and read out twice more, the second reading slower, by a girl of seventeen who is not paid.**

- **What it was:** an instrument of the twelfth of the fourth month, nine pages, Marek Kest's own name at the top of the first, whose effect was that the district's flood authority in respect of the spring works at the third-of-the-way mark should be discharged by the Company working the Slade Cut; in force; guaranteeing every future flood on that cut by never asking anybody twice; with the clause that takes about sixty children under sixteen in on the third page and the clause that says no child may be sued on the fourth, and **both honest, and the second does not stop the first and does not need to.**
- **What the withdrawal says it costs:** that from that morning the bank at the third-of-the-way mark is held by Marek Kest alone, out of a wage of seventeen shillings and a penny the week, until a person is named on a list of works; **and that the district's flood authority in respect of those works is undispensed and has been since the first week of the first month, and that there is no office in this district with power to give it to anybody, because the keeper gave it away in writing in the first month to two people without asking either, and he was one of them, and she was right to, and it cannot be got back.**
- **And the sentence he said out loud in front of about thirty people rather than in a room with a stove in it:** the fourth month of next year is when it rains on that cut; **if he has not got somebody on a list of works by then, he has put about sixty children on a bank on his own, by himself, in a city, and there is no form in this empire for asking them whether they would rather it was me.**
- **And the dating, which is the engineering of it:** the withdrawal takes effect on the second of the fifth month of next year and not before, and on that day the first season of a list of works begins, **and the two are one document and not two, and there is no morning in between, and that is the only reason it is not the worst thing he has ever done.**
- **The second exception** — the ford, the nine stones and the two depth posts, held for one year at forty-five pence a day on five men and a boy out of his own wage — **ended on the same morning because nobody came to the review, and the paper says that is what it does, and nobody in that bay was under any obligation to get on a cart for four hundred and thirty miles to keep it alive.** Hob Bray has still not put nine stones back in the throat of that ford and nobody has asked him to and there is no list that says he must.

### The Rotation, As Established On The Page

- **What it is:** about forty-one persons, in rotation, one season each, at forty-five pence a day, on a list of works, with a day stated against every name, a right to give it up on one day's notice in the person's own hand, a review at the end of each term, and a reader on the same list at the same rate.
- **How it was made:** one question, the same question, in about nine words, asked at about nine doorways a week, in the ordinary way, by people who can be asked why, between the seventh of the seventh month and the second of the fifth month of next year — about thirty weeks. **Not a list made once. A thing that has to keep being done.**
- **The first name:** Kem Ostry, thirty-one, a carter with no employer of record, who had asked on the fifteenth of the seventh month to be asked last and was not. **He was not thanked, did not want to be, and about nine people in that bay looked at the chair and the thanking stopped, and nothing about it is on the page.**
- **The second name:** Halla Wray, fifty-one, foreman, who has put her own name on a page for the first time in nine years and **refused to state the day herself.**
- **The reader:** Tavi Venn, seventeen, engaged in writing from the first of the fourth month of next year, four days a week, forty-five piece a day, a term, a copy, and the same notice as every other person. **She asked for the written engagement herself and had to ask twice.**
- **The refusals:** about nineteen, with reasons, entered with days, **not argued with and not asked a second time**, of whom about nine cannot read a paragraph and about four of those nine have not been asked again and are not going to be.
- **Never asked:** about four people who cannot read a paragraph, and a woman of about seventy-one at the fourth house at the north of the ford, **who is being asked at her own door four times a year, as she asked in writing, and who has said the same reason twice and will not say it a third time.**
- **The consequence the volume does not solve, in its own words, on its last page:** about nineteen people behind that bank said no and are behind it now, and about four people who cannot read a paragraph were never asked, and **a rotation of about forty-one people of whom nine cannot read a paragraph is not a duty, it is a notice**, and the only thing standing between the two is a girl of seventeen who is now paid and can be told to stop.

### The Sorn Enrolment, And The Second Book

**Entered at a waystation on the northern marches about four hundred and thirty miles the other way, in the register of things offered to border households and not answered by them, House Sorn's border law, second section, on the return of the carrier, the eighth of the seventh month. A number in the top left-hand corner in a hand about nine hundred years old. No seal. No name at the foot.**

- Offered: a water guarantee under the fire-and-water device, to an unincorporated company working a public cut in a district named below, and to such persons as are of that company at the time the work is done.
- Offered at: no charge. Carriage: none required. **Answer of the household: none, there being no household to answer, the thing having been offered to a body.**
- The station notes that the same form was offered to the border households of that station in the second month of this year, that they answered it and refused it, that their refusal is entered in the same register **at a number above this one**, and that theirs was answered by persons and this was not.
- **The offer stands. It is not withdrawn by the person offering it. It may be taken up at any time afterwards by a person answering in its terms. A person answering in those terms would be the head of the body, and there is none, and the station has not been asked to certify that a head exists and does not certify it.**
- **What it is: the second book in two months in which a House has had to put something about itself in writing.** The first is a book about a hundred and forty years old with a crack in the spine, at a chamber about two hundred miles south of the table, and it has a **person's** name at the foot of the entry and four sentences of his reason and one sentence in the first person. The second has a House's name and a number and no name. **Vaunt Oris is not on the page in this batch, is not named, and is not asked to do anything; the copy that was carried north on the thirtieth of the sixth month is the thing that was enrolled, and he has not been told and is not going to be.**

### The Two Courts, Still Not Joined

**The order of the court sitting in room four of the eighteenth day of the seventh month of last year gives a hearing and does not say when. The finding of the district court of Mosswake of the ninth day of the eighth month last year says when. The two are nine days out of order with each other. Neither court has read the other. They are not joined on a page anywhere in Volume 03 and they are not joined in this batch.**

**On the sixteenth day of the eleventh month of last year the district court was asked, in writing, whether it would treat the second of the fifth month of next year as a date of its own so that a foreman could act on it in a room, and answered no, and refused, in a form with a reason and no why:**

- This court cannot hold a term in another person's paper.
- The order of the eighteenth of the seventh gives a hearing and does not say when; the finding of the ninth of the eighth says when.
- **The two are nine days out of order with each other. This court has not read the first and that court has not read this, and there is no form in this Empire for one court to ask another court what it has found, and this court has tried to write one four times in nine years and has not sent it and has written *why not* against it three times.**
- And, in a magistrate's own hand, outside the form: if the two halves are ever put on the same table in a room with a witness in it, this court will read both and will not say which is wrong, **because this court does not know, and a court that does not know should say so in a form with a day on it, and that is what I have done.**
- **And the answer to what happens instead, given by a book-keeper of thirty-eight in a bay: the date is going to be kept by somebody walking, and it has to be a person who is not paid, or it is a favour, and a favour has no notice.** **The door to Auremar is still a door.**

### Figures Named In Volume 03 Batch 0005

**Forty-five pence a day on five men and a boy. About ninety days a season. Three pounds fifteen and threepence for a season. Three pounds eleven and threepence for nineteen days, out of seventeen shillings and a penny the week, called held. Seven shillings and sixpence is two days of that crew. One shilling and sixpence a day is the guild's rate for a barrow of stone, a man and a boy; ninety days is a hundred and thirty-five shillings, which is six pounds fifteen shillings. Four shillings a year is the Company's whole money and is the rent of a stone store. A reader's day is between four pence and a shilling. About ninety-one households and about sixty children under sixteen. About four hundred and forty foot of outer face, eleven feet high, about nine acres, about thirty-one households behind it, about one hundred and forty feet of ditch and a scour. About nine hundred and forty names in the Company's book, nine hands, about sixty years, no heading, about four hundred dead; the ninth hundred and fortieth is of a person dead about twenty-two years; a fifth hand in about sixty years. The north bay is thirty-one foot by nineteen; the south end of the same store about eleven feet by nineteen. About sixty wanted, about forty-one yes, about nineteen no, about four never asked. Nine doorways a week, about thirty weeks. Four hundred and thirty miles, four times on the page unhedged in this batch. Nine hundred and forty people a magistrate made answerable; about four hundred she chose not to hold and has refused to name; about a hundred and forty files. Nineteen pounds three and fourpence; six pounds and eightpence for the quarter; about six pounds on the average; about a penny a day; about fifty-seven pounds of a tally never claimed; four hundred and eleven on the far side; three hundred and eighty-eight crossings. Fifty-four filed copies; thirty-one with a name and a door, twenty-two seen and nine told; eleven with a word and no name; twelve back from the post; six never made; eighteen returns that did not come back. About two hundred and forty copies sold in that district, and no list of the buyers. A hundred and forty years of a House's fire-and-water licence on one reach. About eleven households on a dyers' lane and about forty people in eleven houses and four other houses in three other districts. Eleven days of a letter going between two clerks; about eleven months. Four, one hundred and ninety days asked for and one thousand two hundred and three agreed to. One levy, one shop's credit, one job in one town, and it is not hers.**

**The hedge audit for this batch, whole-word counts in the ten files: *about nine* 113, *about four* 75, *about forty* 33, *about thirty* 47, *about twenty* 13, *about nineteen* 4, *about sixty* 30, and thirty-one unhedged *four hundred and thirty*, which are not instances of any of the above.**

### Divergences Between The Cards And The Page, Volume 03 Batch 0005

**The ten cards were written before the prose by the run that wrote the chapters, in the way Batches 0001 to 0004 wrote their own. This section replaces the placeholder. The page governs in every case. None of these is a canon change, and none of them contradicts an inherited fact.**

1. **The card for Chapter 0147 said the carter would give the fifth question of Volume 02 word for word, having carried it since a gate. The page does not do that, and the reason is on the page and is better.** Chapter 0100 has it: **a girl of eleven asked Tavi Venn four questions and a fifth at a Reed Cut gate, and the fifth was hers, not Tavi's, and Tavi did not answer it.** A carter of thirty-one could not have heard it. **The page gives him his own question in his own words — who is going to be standing there when I want to know what this paper says — and Tavi Venn then identifies it as the same question near enough, and says she has been carrying the girl's version since the twentieth of the twelfth month of last year, and says that he got his from standing in a yard in the cold and so did the girl.** The rotation's operative clause is therefore in his words and is a convergence, not a quotation, and **no chapter claims the two are the same.**
2. **The card for Chapter 0147 also said the reader's rate is forty-five pence and that the reader is in the rotation, and the page keeps both, and adds the reason the rate is not a reader's rate at all**, which the card did not ask for and which is Chapter 0149's spine: **a person who is not paid is doing a favour and a favour has no notice, and that is the reason a reader cannot be paid and not the money.**
3. **The card for Chapter 0143 promised the day a chair fixed would produce a resolution refused and a count. The page produces the count and also produces a hand at the west end of the room at about the ninth hour offering nine untidy words, which no card asked for and which is the hinge of the whole batch**: a carter of thirty-one asks to be asked last, and a chair of sixty-four writes it down untidied and says she does not know what to do with it and is not going to find out that morning.
4. **The card for Chapter 0142 said the rule would be used a sixth time with a card that earns it. The page does not use it at all, and the not-using is the finding**: **one is not available to you — you cannot write it on forty-one people who have not been asked, because the instrument says so and you wrote it.** The rule is used in Chapter 0148 instead, as planned. **The prompt permitted a sixth use with a card that earns it; the page used the rule's own non-applicability instead and recorded that as the first correct use of the rule anybody has made in a year, which is a stronger result and is in the file.**
5. **The card for Chapter 0150 asked for the final image with a reader who is not paid. The page pays her and says so on the bank.** The divergence from `outline/volume-03.md` is declared before the prose, in the cards file, and paid for on the page: **she was not paid for four years and is paid now, a reader who is paid can be told to stop, that is the first arrangement in this matter in which the person who turns up can be told to stop, and it is the whole of what the volume bought and it is not a wall.** The rest of the image is kept whole.
6. **The card for Chapter 0150 did not name the first holder. The page names him: Kem Ostry, thirty-one**, a carter with no employer of record who has been *the man and a boy* in a guild's phrase for about six weeks with his name in no column anywhere. **That is the only new named figure in the batch**, he is bound for the rest of the series, and he is a working person with an independent want who is not a villain and is not a mouth for a power he does not hold.
7. **The card for Chapter 0145 did not bring Nell Kest on. It did, and it paid for it**: a lane, a shop, one honest sentence about what the work has cost her family, a refused column entered in a book that is not hers, and a brother who has not written since the second month and a sister who has not answered and is entitled to that. **She is not a messenger, not a device and not a reconciliation, and no blank heir, no archive, no will, no sealed page and no bundle appears in the chapter.**
8. **The card for Chapter 0144 said something must come back from the north or the batch must say plainly that nothing has. The page brings it back, and what comes back is an enrolment and not the answer anybody wanted**, and the finding is the batch's institutional one about lists with an end in them and lists without one.
9. **The card for Chapter 0146 asked the volume to say in plain words why the thirty-one do not get a seventh column. The page says why twice** — once in the box, and once in a sentence the clerk flags as hers and not part of the reason — **and gives them a sixth space with no heading over it and one line in it, which the card had not proposed, and which is the same protection a foreman found at a gate nine months earlier by accident and which nobody told her about.**
10. **The card for Chapter 0142 named a person to ask what a rotation is for and required her not to answer. The page has her answer it, at about the sixth hour, in about four sentences, as a fact and not as advice, and she says so twice, and the answer is that about forty-nine people have not been asked and somebody else is going to ask them in about nine weeks and it is not going to be him.** That is the pull into Chapter 0143 and it is better than the card.
11. **The card for Chapter 0148 required the price of his suffering to be put in a room and refused to be softened. The page does that, twice, from her, at length, and adds the thing no card asked for: that the price of nine weeks of his wage is not the wage.** The wage is the cost of the holding. The price is that **for nine weeks there is exactly one name against that bank and it is his, and if the bank goes in the fourth month of next year it goes onto one household in a city four hundred and thirty miles away, and nobody in ninety-one households will be able to find him and he will be able to find all of them.**
12. **Two things in the cards that the page did not do, recorded so that no one goes looking for them.** The cards proposed a form for the reader's wage of four pence; the page has four pence as a *copy* and a shilling as a *question at a table*, and the reader is on the rotation at forty-five pence, which is the Company's own day rate, and the finding is that there is no money anywhere in ninety-one households at all. And the cards proposed the fourth envelope in Tamsin Rook's drawer be touched; the page does not open it, does not send it, and says it is four lines long and has been there since the first month of the year before last, and that is all.

### The Bounds On Volume 03, Checked In Ten Files And Holding

No reading of any instrument is performed by anybody in Chapters 141–150, and the only two readings in the manuscript remain Chapter 8 and Chapter 40. No certification is granted, no register of persons who read is entered, Marek Kest has no office and no fund, and his bar is discharged. The counter-mark reads nothing, decides nothing, imposes no tether, and does not know where the ford is, and the wage is the price and not the mark. The word *exception* is used of three instruments and nothing else and is not used of the Venn certification, which has five operative terms and is never said to have four. The substituted phrase *the present holder* does not appear in Chapters 141–150 and no fifth instance of anything is added, and the guarantee is not paraphrased into a new order and is not quoted. Vaunt Oris is not on the page, is not named and is not asked to do anything, and his House's concession on that reach is lapsed with his name at the foot of the entry and a copy of that entry enrolled in a border register four hundred and thirty miles north of which he has not been told. Seryn Oris is not named, not present, and credited with nothing she did not do; the counsel of the First Seat is unnamed; the three Orises are never merged. The two documents are not named anywhere in the batch, the quarterly return is still in no reply, and Hallis Dren's return of the eleventh of the tenth is dormant, armed, and known to nobody in the story. Tamsin Rook's fourth envelope is there, unsent and unopened, with three others. The undertaking of the fourth of the first month is live, is not retired, is not amended, and is not exercised in this batch. The rule of the twelfth month of last year is used once, a sixth time, in Chapter 0148. The thirty-one of Mosswake arithmetic is untouched and no figure against it is altered and the three thirty-ones are three figures in three places. The fifth heading of the register of pending duties is *Copy sold*. The file of the sixth year in the embankment corridor is not opened, nothing is learned about it, it is not turned into a discovery, and the only new thing about it is that its not-opening was witnessed by a person from a bay and written down, which is a record and not a discovery. Lowcross is open, unpaid, nineteen pounds three and fourpence, nobody liable, and no chapter pays, forgives, funds or resolves it. The girl of eleven at the Reed Cut gate is unnamed, is given no reason for not reading, is not a device, is not a symbol and is owed nothing, and the page states in one sentence what was done to her. Raff Ordway, sixteen, is not in this batch; the boy of fifteen is not in this batch and no chapter states that the leg is permanently injured; the two are never equated. Rosamund Crane, eleven, is not in this batch and reads. Bel Skell is not the girl of eleven. Tarn Skell is not identified as living or dead. The mother called Nery is not Nery Caudle. The two sealed pages stay shut with no third refusal, the three red things are never joined in a sentence, and the Ashfall bundle is not opened and is not named. What Ilyra Kest found in the Ashfall archive is not answered, is not referred to as a question, and is not advanced in one line, and the page gives the route — a run number in a notary's table and a category of person who may be made a party — and says it is a route and not an answer. No romance begins and none is started, and the paper that puts Tamsin Rook in a bay is not the reason and both of them know it and neither says it. The four bound obligations of `outline/series.md` are all paid: Burden-Bearer in Chapter 0150 with a named cost, Tamsin's refusal to romanticise in Chapters 0138, 0148 and 0150, Magistrate Rell's own flood records in Chapter 0136, and the Kingsway Road Guild and the Sorn border households in Chapters 0137, 0144 and 0150.

# THE REVIEW REPAIR OF VOLUME 03 BATCH 0005

**This section is dated after `state/current.md` and supersedes it wherever the two differ, and it supersedes `The Volume 03 Batch 0005 Word-Count Record` on every figure it repeats. The batch was not restarted, no chapter was rewritten, and no fact of the world, no date, no figure of speech and no plot point was changed. Nine findings came back from `logs/next-0001.review.log`. Eight were fixed. One was not, because every file in it is controller-owned.**

## The Two Defects That Would Have Cost A Volume

**One: the phase of record was fourth in the dispatcher's order.** Both `scripts/novel_runner.sh` and `.github/workflows/novels.yml` take the **first** `PROMPT.md` in `find … | sort` order that has no `.done`, `.blocked` or `.retired` marker. Four were open after the closing batch: `workspace/continuation/next-0001/` (the generic seed this run was executing, whose text still said *after the completed phase batch-0003*), `workspace/volume-03/batch-0004/`, `workspace/volume-03/batch-0005/`, and `workspace/volume-03/volume-close/`. The last is the correct next phase and it sorted **fourth**. The next dispatch would have re-run Chapters 131–140 over finished prose. **Fixed by adding `.retired` to `batch-0004/` and `batch-0005/`.** `.retired` and not `.done`, because `PHASE_SYSTEM.md` says only the controller may mark a phase done or blocked, and because `.retired` is the marker the controller both honours and creates itself when a prompt declares itself retired. **The controller writes `.done` to `continuation/next-0001/` when this run completes, and `volume-close/PROMPT.md` is then the first and only open prompt. This is the third occurrence of a prompt sorting ahead of the phase of record, after `workspace/volume-02/batch-0004/` and `workspace/continuation/next/`, and the standing instruction is that a writing phase checks for a stale or duplicate prompt of its own batch before it ends and never creates a second next phase.**

**Two: the volume-close prompt could not be executed.** Its read list named five card files as worth reading whole (486,049 bytes) and six state files (2,084,057 bytes) with three outlines and seven chapters: **2,785,505 bytes, about 710,000 tokens.** It now names the sections to read, states the four files that are legitimately read whole (26,608 bytes), gives the measured size of the card-file sections it wants (33,253 bytes from Batch 0005's seven named sections and 30,947 bytes of closing-divergences sections from Batches 0001–0004), and gives a total budget of **about 330,000 bytes, about 82,000 tokens**, with `grep -n` named as the sanctioned way to locate a heading. It also carries three craft locks into the outline it is about to write, and it forbids the audit from appending a seventh full restatement of the volume to a state file.

## The Prose Repairs, Chapter By Chapter, With The Measurement

**Word counts, re-measured with `sed 's/[[:space:]]*$//' file | wc -w` after the repair.** 141: 3,785. 142: 3,458. 143: **3,147** (was 3,123). 144: **3,594** (was 3,542). 145: 2,899. 146: 2,998. 147: **3,367** (was 3,351). 148: **4,050** (was 4,050). 149: **3,405** (was 3,396). 150: 4,597. **Batch 35,300, was 35,199; volume 176,097, was 175,996; manuscript 562,253, was 562,152. The whole repair added 101 words and removed none, and every added word is in the sentence-lengthening needed to break a repeated refrain. The error in the earlier disclosure is corrected here: Chapter 148 was never inside the 3,200–4,000 band at 4,050, so the count of chapters inside the band is five (141, 142, 144, 147, 149) and not six, and Chapter 143 is fifty-three words under the floor and not seventy-seven.**

**Bold, the finding that emphasis had become the default.** Share of chapter text in bold, before and after: **141 28→28, 142 36→32, 143 34→32, 144 41→26, 145 28→24, 146 31→28, 147 27→23, 148 41→37, 149 52→40, 150 36→37.** Lines carrying bold, out of all non-blank lines, before and after: 141 30→30, 142 34→32, 143 36→32, 144 43→31, 145 31→28, 146 35→30, 147 48→31, 148 44→35, 149 46→36, 150 39→39. **Sixty-two bold runs were removed across seven chapters and every one removed was a restatement of something said in the same scene, a narratorial flag telling the reader that a sentence matters, or emphasis on a line whose neighbour already carried the weight.** No panel lost its typography, and no sentence the volume turns on lost its emphasis: a company cannot say no, a count is a thing you can act on and a yes is not, a list with a term in it is a chain with a door in it, a person who reads and is not in the rotation is a person who cannot be told to stop, a form with no name on it got answered faster than a form with a man's name on it, a list of works has an end in it and a list of persons does not, an exception is a piece of paper, the mark is not the price and the wage is the price, and the four statements the reader of Volume 02 has waited nine chapters for are all still marked. **What is left in 0148, 0149 and 0150 is concentrated in the blockquoted forms and in the load-bearing sentences, and that residue is recorded as it stands rather than cut further, because a further cut would take the emphasis off the sentences and leave the restatements.**

**The refrain.** **"Nobody said anything"** ran eight times across Chapters 0141–0150 — 0143 twice, 0144 three times, 0147 twice, 0149 once. It now appears **once**, at Chapter 0143 line 21, where it is followed by the door doing what it does and where the other seven instances were formula. The other seven were replaced with what was actually happening: a man at the back half raising his hand and putting it down again; a bay working out that a border law has handed it a piece of paper; a bay going on being a bay and the forge going on being the forge; the forge starting at that hour and four people hearing it and nobody using it as an excuse to look away; a gap of nine seconds in a yard; four people at a mill wall not moving; and a bench at the back going quiet first. **No figure was changed, because the refrain's neighbours carried the nine.**

**Case.** `this Empire` was capitalised **twelve** times in Volume 03 against `this empire` **184** times, and the lowercase form is used inside blockquoted forms as well, so it was never a register convention. **All twelve are now lower case**, three of them written in this batch (Chapter 0146 twice, Chapter 0149 once) and nine in Chapters 0131, 0132, 0133, 0136 twice, 0137 and 0139 twice. **Volume 03 now has no capital-E empire in it and `this Empire` returns nothing across `chapters/`.**

**Markup.** Chapter 0149 line 131 nested `*why not*` inside a `***` run and Chapter 0142 line 129 nested a `**` run inside a `**` run; neither would render as intended. **Both repaired by keeping the outer emphasis and dropping the inner pair.** No other unbalanced delimiter exists in the ten chapters: the only odd `**` counts are in Chapter 0144's multi-paragraph blockquoted form, where an italic opened on one line closes on another, and that is the form's own structure and is unchanged.

**`wrote`** ran 58 times in the batch, 14 of them in Chapter 0141. **Chapter 0141 is a book-keeper writing things down and the word is its subject; three narration instances were varied and thirteen were left, because a book-keeper's chapter should say wrote.**

**`about nine` was not touched and the reason is the point.** It appears 105 times across the ten chapters. It is the narrator's precision tic, and it is also load-bearing: the count of people in a bay, the length of a season, the number of households behind a bank, the number of people who cannot read a paragraph and the width of a store are all figures the state files record verbatim and all of them are built on it. **Varying the number to make a page read differently would have made the record false, so the tic is bound instead of varied — about eight per chapter in Volume 04 — and the bound is in `state/current.md` and in the locks section the volume-close phase must write into `outline/volume-04.md`.**

## What Was Verified Clean And Was Not Re-Checked

All ten chapters exist as finished prose. No meta language, TODO, placeholder or outline language in any of them. No calendar month name and no weekday name in any of the ten, and all twenty-eight `may` hits are the modal and all four `sat` hits are the past tense of *sit*. **Zero duplicate paragraphs and zero duplicate sentences across the ten chapters after the repair**, apart from two form lines that are meant to repeat — a clause of the instrument in Chapters 0142 and 0149, and *Answered: no.* in Chapters 0146 and 0149. The budget breaches are real and disclosed: 145 and 146 in the aftermath band, 143 fifty-three under the floor, 148 fifty over the band, and 150 397 over the about-4,200 ceiling as the volume close. **No new final enemy; Vaunt Oris is not named and has not been told.** No reading of any instrument is performed in the batch and the only two readings in the manuscript remain Chapter 8 and Chapter 40. The three exceptions remain three and the word *exception* is not used of the Venn certification.

## The Finding That Was Not Fixed, And Cannot Be

**The review was run by the writer.** `scripts/novel_runner.sh:273` and `.github/workflows/novels.yml:64` call `opencode run --agent novel-reviewer`, and `.opencode/agent/novel-reviewer.md` declares `mode: subagent`, so opencode refuses the flag and falls back to the default agent, which is the writer. **This repair pass is therefore a self-review, and the fixes above were chosen by the same author who wrote the prose they correct, which is the weakest possible form of review and is stated here rather than left to be discovered.** The fix is one word, `mode: primary`, in a controller-owned file. `state/phase-ledger.json` is the second controller-owned file that is permanently stale and nothing in the repository reads or writes it. Both are recorded in `state/current.md` and neither is touched here.

# THE VOLUME 03 AUDIT

**Run on the twenty-sixth of September 2026 by the volume-close phase of Volume 03, which was the only phase Volume 03 got. It is the audit `PHASE_SYSTEM.md` requires under *Volume audit*, and it is placed at the end of this file because this file is append-only downward and the Volume 03 material has been growing at the bottom since Batch 0001; the newest-first rule applies to `state/current.md` and not to this one, and a review on the same day established that `state/open-threads.md` and `state/chapter-summaries.md` are append-only downward as well and are read at the bottom — the correction is in *The Review Repair Of The Volume 03 Volume-Close Handover* at the end of this file. This is a reading. It changes no chapter, it introduces no canon, and it closes no thread. Its two companion outputs are `outline/volume-04.md` and the section below it, *What A Volume 04 Writer Inherits, In One Page*.**

**What was read, and what was not.** `outline/volume-03.md` whole; the `Volume audit` section of `PHASE_SYSTEM.md` and nothing else in it; the Volume 04 section of `outline/series.md`; the resolution, power-and-relationship and final-image material of `outline/volume-01.md` and `outline/volume-02.md`; the named sections of `state/current.md`, `state/open-threads.md`, `state/batch-summary.md`, `state/character-state.md` and `state/chapter-summaries.md` that the phase prompt names; the five card files by section and never whole; and Chapters 145, 149, 150 and 100 entire. **Thirty-one of the fifty chapters were not re-read in full for this audit.** Where a check below is a negative — that two of the five thirty-one figures are never set side by side, that no scene gives a character a duty without the cost of stopping it — the audit says what it actually checked and does not claim a proof it did not make. Every negative that *was* machine-checked was machine-checked over all fifty files and the method is given.

## 1. The Outline Against The Produced Summaries

**Verdict: the volume delivered the outline and exceeded it in one direction, and the excess is the most useful thing this audit has produced. Six places where the page went somewhere the outline did not send it are recorded; one is the declared and paid final-image divergence and needs no new work; and three pieces of the outline were simply not used, which is a finding about a self-authored plan and not a defect in the volume.**

**What the outline asked for and got, checked item by item.** The **central pressure** — whether a cooperative can be made to hold a public duty without making every child in it a debtor — is asked in the first week of the first month and answered twice, in Chapter 0127 in mud in a mill yard, and in Chapter 0150 in four lines on the back of a sheet. The **starting state** holds: the fifth term's schedule is on the rail in Chapter 0110, the bar is discharged, the watch is entered in one sentence, Tamsin Rook's office that does not exist is inherited, Lowcross is nineteen pounds three and fourpence and unpaid, the thirty-one are thirty-one, and Nell Kest and Bren Kest are in the volume and get a lane and a shop. The **starting relationships** hold, all three of them, and the letter of the sixteenth of the eighth is still unanswered on the last page. The **starting power level** holds and is exactly as specified: Chapter 0105 is the training, out of an afternoon, a reading of a file, with no fund, in a room with a person in it who is not the person being trained, and it says on the page *it is not a certification, and no certification exists*. The **one rule** is kept in every respect and is verified by machine over all fifty files: the two documents are not named anywhere in Volume 03, the quarterly return is in no reply, the substituted phrase *the present holder* does not occur in Volume 03 at all so no fifth instance could be added and no chain could be written, the two sealed pages are not named, the three red things are not named, the counting-house mark is not converted, the Venn ruling is never said to have four terms. The **second rule** is established and kept. All **five escalation steps** are on the page. All **four bound obligations** are paid, and each is paid from the page and not from the outline: **Burden-Bearer in Chapter 0150** with a cost named as his own labour and his own liability and nothing else; **Tamsin Rook's refusal to romanticise in Chapters 0138, 0148 and 0150**, three times, at length, and in Chapter 0148 the price of his suffering is put in a room and refused to be softened and is then complicated by the finding that the price of nine weeks of his wage is not the wage; **Magistrate Rell's own flood records in Chapter 0136**, about a hundred and forty files in her own hand in her own press, read as a set, three categories, about nine hundred and forty made answerable and about four hundred not, entered as a finding of fact about her own practice on her own motion, naming no person, not appealable by anybody, and said four times on the page to be not a discovery and not a seam; and **the Kingsway Road Guild and the Sorn border households in Chapters 0137, 0144 and 0150**. The **concrete resolution** and its four promises are paid in Chapter 0146, Chapter 0149 and Chapter 0150 and are taken up at **6** below. The **next-volume question** is opened as a route in one paragraph in Chapter 0150 and is not advanced. The **final chapter image** is on the page whole, with the one declared divergence, and **the divergence needs no new work beyond this line: the outline says a girl of eleven is read to by somebody who is not paid, the page pays her, and the page says so in the same breath as the reading, and the reason is the volume's own argument and not a softening of it.**

**Six places the page went somewhere else.**

1. **The rotation is the volume, and the outline asked for an arrangement.** The outline's fifth step asks for *a cooperative accepts a rotating duty with a stated term, a review date and a right of refusal in it, and a body is made to be able to stop*. The page produces a theory of what an arrangement is: that a rotation is not a list but one question asked sixty times in sixty doorways by a person who can be asked why, with a sixth line that a person who says no is not asked again; that a duty that stops is not a duty that survives stopping and the difference is a name entered before the term ends and a name cannot be entered before a person has been asked; that a term with a review is a duty with a door in it and the door is a person and if the person does not come the duty stops and the water does not stop; that a list of works has an end in it and a list of persons does not, in eleven words; that about nineteen of about sixty will say no and the nineteen are still behind the bank; and that a rotation of about forty-one people of whom nine cannot read a paragraph is a notice and not a duty. **None of that is in the outline and all of it is what the volume is.**
2. **The reader is the volume's second subject and the outline does not know it exists.** The outline's final image contains a girl who cannot read a list and a reader and nothing else about reading. The page made *and who is it that reads it to the ones who cannot read it* — a question a girl of eleven asked at a gate in Volume 02 and nobody answered — into the volume's second question, and answered it with a written engagement at forty-five pence a day, a term, a copy, the same notice as everybody else, a thanks withdrawn in a book with a reason, and the finding that a person who is not paid is doing a favour and a favour has no notice. **The page then diverged from the outline by paying her, and the divergence is the argument and not a softening, and this is the single best thing the page did that the outline did not ask for.**
3. **The reversal is a drafting history and not a moral one.** The outline's midpoint says the instrument *saves a named household*. The page's first exception of the twelfth of the fourth month discharges the district's flood authority onto the Company, guarantees every future flood by never asking anybody twice, and takes about sixty children under sixteen with it — and the household that watches it happen is a bay of about thirty who came to see whether being helped works, and the finding is one a person can only make by watching a household that has just been helped. The wrong end of the instrument was not a moral error and it was a **drafting** error, carried for eleven weeks, and the reason is that he could not stand on a towpath for eleven weeks, and the end that works is the end with about sixty people in it. **The page is better and the finding is the same.**
4. **Vaunt Oris split the instrument instead of refusing it.** The outline says he *refuses a false reading* and a concession is lost. On the page, in Chapter 0139, he **refuses the first limb and certifies the second** — the sentence true of a book and false of a cut — and the concession of about a hundred and forty years of fire-and-water licence on one reach is entered lapsed with his name at the foot and one sentence in the first person. He is not a villain, is not converted, does not apologise, does not explain himself, and is not asked to do anything else. **The refinement is that the price was exact and named and not moralised, and that is a harder thing to write than a refusal.**
5. **The Seryn thread got quieter, not louder.** The outline's fourth step has *Seryn's wing write again*. On the page the counsel of the First Seat stays unnamed, **Seryn Oris is not named anywhere in Volume 03**, and a foreman of fifty-one declines a notice of a seat in nine words and a magistrate answers a seat in a form with a reason and no why. **The page is stricter than the plan in the direction the whole series wants, and it is a divergence, and it is the right one.**
6. **Three pieces of the outline were not used, and a Volume 04 writer should not assume they are still owed.** The **old bridge and the road up to the Salt Teeth**, named as later locations, are not on the page: the road that gets walked north is the towpath to a Sorn waystation four hundred and thirty miles the other way, and the bridge that is live in Volume 03 is **Lowcross**, which the outline's own starting state puts at nineteen pounds three and fourpence and unpaid. **The Cinder Court at the Crown Kiln**, named among the major factions with its registrar and its judge, does not appear at all; the full sitting of the eighteenth of the seventh was never staged in Volume 02 and Volume 03 did not stage it, and the registrar and the judge are not on the page. **The Chancery of the Roll**, named among the major factions, does not appear as an institution; the Docket of Powers' three rooms and the Received Room do. **None of these is a debt and none may be entered as one.** The Salt Teeth are the setting of Volume 12 and the Cinder Court's own rooms are the setting of Volume 05.

**The finding this audit exists to hand on.** A self-authored volume outline written before the prose is a plan and not a contract, and the three passages where the page beat the plan were all the same move: **the plan asked for an outcome and the page found the question underneath the outcome and gave the question to a named working person who is not a lawyer.** Halla Wray found it in a shed on the back of a return. Rennick Adley wrote it in a book with no heading. Doreth Ferrow said it before anybody spoke. A carter of thirty-one said no in eleven seconds and gave the operative clause. **Volume 04's outline should be written expecting the same, and should plan in the people rather than the outcomes.**

## 2. Unresolved Promises And Mysteries

**Verdict: nothing was closed, nothing was quietly dropped, and the eleven items the closing batch's notes name are a floor and not a ceiling. Twenty-six threads are open at the close of Chapter 150 and every one has a Volume 04 or later target or an explicit em dash. The four items paid in this volume are paid and are not debts.**

Paid, and not debts: the **withdrawal of the first exception** (dated the twenty-fourth of the ninth month of last year, effective the second of the fifth month of next year, read out in nine sentences in front of about thirty people) — `—`, it is paid and it is not undone. The **second exception, the ford** (ended on the day because nobody came to the review, which is what its paper says) — `—`, it is paid. The **Corvin Dray trial and plea** of Volume 02 — `—`. **Nell Kest's fourth column** and her **one honest sentence** about what the work has cost their family — paid in Chapter 0145; what is unpaid in that line is her brother's letter, below.

**Open, with a target.**

1. **The Lowcross bill.** Nineteen pounds three and fourpence, unpaid, nobody liable; six pounds and eightpence committed for the quarter and about six pounds on the average of the four quarters; **no line for a bridge in that fund and there has not been one in nineteen years**; about a penny a day; four hundred and eleven people on the far side; three hundred and eighty-eight crossings in the district's return for four weeks, which are two figures and are never the same; about fifty-seven pounds of a tally gone in over nineteen years and never claimed. **A further application may be made on seven days' notice and the works, the household and the seat may each apply again.** → **Volume 04 and after.**
2. **The thirty-one of Mosswake and the seventh column.** Still thirty-one; no seventh column, refused in a form with a reason and no why; the fourth column reads *next review: none named* thirty-one times; the fifth heading is *Copy sold*; a sixth ruled space with one line in it and no heading over it, which is worth nothing at law. **31 + 11 + 12 = 54, 54 + 6 = 60, twenty-two seen and nine told, six copies never made, eighteen returns that did not come back, and nobody adds the six to the eighteen.** → **Volume 04 and after, and it is still the largest unpaid debt of the series.**
3. **The eighteen returns that did not come back and the six copies never made**, which are two different facts and are never added together. → **Volume 04 and after.**
4. **The fourth column, reading *next review: none named* thirty-one times, and the fifth heading, *Copy sold*,** and **the sixth space with one line in it and no heading over it.** → **Volume 04 and after.**
5. **The file of the sixth year**, shut between two others in the fourth press in the embankment corridor, not opened at any point in Volume 03 after Chapter 0120, and nothing learned about it; the only new thing about it is that its not-opening was witnessed by a person from a bay and written down on the back of a form with the day on it, which is a record and not a discovery. **About a hundred and forty files are in that press and Leda Rell's hand is in them.** → **Volume 04 and after, and it may not be turned into a discovery by implication.**
6. **About nine hundred and forty people a magistrate made answerable in nineteen years in about a hundred and forty files, and about four hundred she chose not to hold and has refused to name** — about two hundred and ten who cannot be found, about a hundred and forty who are of a household, and a third category she has never written down, on the grounds that a person entitled to be heard about it would then be able to find her. → **Volume 04**, where she testifies about the river precedent, and after.
7. **The district court's four attempts in nine years to write a form for asking another court what it has found**, none of which was sent, with *why not* written against three of them. → **Volume 04 and after.**
8. **A district court and a high court nine days out of order with each other, neither having read the other,** the order of the eighteenth of the seventh giving a hearing and not saying when and the finding of the ninth of the eighth saying when; the district court has refused, in a form, to treat the second of the fifth month of next year as a date of its own, and has said in a magistrate's own hand that this court does not know and a court that does not know should say so in a form with a day on it. **A later volume may join the two only in a room with a witness in it.** → **Volume 04 and after.**
9. **A seat's carriage offer that has been inoperable for about sixteen months**, open since the sixth month of last year, paying carriage and not attendance, with no form in this empire for compelling a person to be brought to a room; **the substitute is a person walking who is not paid, because a favour has no notice.** → **Volume 04 and after.**
10. **A guarantee under the fire-and-water device standing offered and unanswered at no charge with about sixty children under sixteen inside it**, answerable at any time afterwards by a person answering in its terms, with a labour clause that takes everybody born into the Company and a disclaimer that is true about suing and not true about being in it; **the device has no notary behind it on that reach any more.** → **Volume 04 and after.**
11. **The run number in a notary's table in Auremar, the category of person who may be made a party, and a clerk of thirty-four's own note in her own hand about what a table may certify and may not** — the road to a document in an archive that names a blank heir. **The source leaf does not travel: a written application, a court direction and a recorded custody entry are all three required, and Volume 03 supplied none of the three.** → **Volume 04, which is the volume that answers it.**
12. **What Ilyra Kest found in the Ashfall archive, and why the original document names a blank heir** — still not on the page, not referred to as a question, not advanced in one line. → **Volume 04, and the mid-point of that volume.**
13. **The two documents, still two, four hundred miles apart, in two buildings, with no instrument joining them; the First Seat quarterly return still in no reply; Hallis Dren's return of the eleventh of the tenth still dormant, armed, and known to nobody in the story; only Marek Kest and Tamsin Rook know both exist.** → **Volume 04 for the first two; the dormant return runs to Volume 10.**
14. **The fourth envelope in Tamsin Rook's drawer**, unsent, unopened, there since the first month of the year before last, with three others in it. **Nobody has mentioned it in sixteen months and he has never asked and she has never offered.** → **Volume 04 and after, and the conversation about protection and secrecy is about this and about everything else.**
15. **The two sealed pages, shut, with two refusals between them and no third,** which may not be staged. → **Volume 04 and after.**
16. **The Ashfall bundle**, unopened, and in Volume 03 not named. → **Volume 04 and after.**
17. **The three red things** — the red stitch in Ivo Harker's tin, the thread in the fold of the sealed page, the working stitch in the Ashfall bundle — three different things, never joined in a sentence, and in Volume 03 not named at all. → **— as a mystery; a permanent structural lock, not a debt.**
18. **The border enrolment four hundred and thirty miles north**, in House Sorn's border law, with a number in the corner and no name at the foot, recording that the answer of the household is *none, there being no household to answer, the thing having been offered to a body*, with the Sorn border households' own refusal entered at a number above it, theirs answered by persons and this not. **Vaunt Oris has not been told and is not going to be.** → **Volume 04 and the reader who has been looking for this for a long time.**
19. **A factor of forty-six has asked in writing, for the first time in nineteen years, for the name of the man who instructs him**, dated the second of the seventh month of last year, went out on the third with the weekly, read out in a bay, **and it has not been answered and the volume closed with it still not coming**; about nine men in three gangs would be stood off over about a fortnight if it has cost him the contract; his list of persons is still blank in a wagon. → **Volume 04 and after.**
20. **The guild's list of persons, still blank,** and the institutional finding that a person on a list of works is on it for a season and comes off and the day they come off is a public day, and a person on a list of persons is on it until a man with a tray changes his mind in a city and tells nobody. → **Volume 04 and after.**
21. **About nineteen of about sixty who refused a rotation, entered with reasons and days, not argued with and not asked a second time, of whom about nine cannot read a paragraph and about four of those nine have not been asked again and are not going to be; **and there is a second and different four — about four people who cannot read a paragraph who were never asked at all, which is its own line on the last page and is not the same four, and the two fours may never be merged**; and a woman of about seventy-one at the fourth house at the north of the ford being asked at her own door four times a year, as she asked in writing, who has said the same reason twice and will not say it a third time.** → **Volume 04 and after, and it is the volume's largest open wound.**
22. **The second season of the rotation, which nobody has agreed to do twice and nobody has been asked to do,** roughly the eighth month of next year, because a season is about ninety days. → **Volume 04's second batch, and the review at the end of the first term is a date stated against a name.**
23. **Nell Kest's brother's letter of the sixteenth of the eighth of last year is still unanswered and she is still entitled to leave it unanswered,** an assessor told her that neither of those is a bookkeeping matter, and nobody in that lane asked about it and she is not going to start. → **Volume 04, and their own scenes.**
24. **The two sealed things that are people:** the woman of about thirty-one at the first house at the north of the ford who refused a name twice and is on the rotation as a description and not a name, and the boy of fifteen who cannot stand on the leg, and Raff Ordway, sixteen, who is not him, and the girl of eleven at the Reed Cut gate, unnamed, who is owed nothing and is given no reason and is not a device. → **— as debts; permanent locks.**
25. **Ostyn Vare has still not answered a letter** (Volume 01, unresolved into Volume 03). → **— ; inherited and unpaid, and a Volume 04 writer may not quietly retire it.**
26. **The Nevin Trost corridor instruction of the ninth of the eleventh of last year**, ninety words, unfiled, in his own hands, and Marek Kest still has not written to him and has written down the reason, which is that the rule he made to protect himself is protecting Trost. → **Volume 04 and after.**

**Two figures that were offered to this audit in its own instructions and are not canon, recorded so that nobody inherits them.** **The Lowcross fund has had no line for a bridge in it for nineteen years, not twenty-nine** — the phrase on the page is *and there has not been one in nineteen years*, and the tally it stands on is nineteen years. **The landing, the weigh-house and the lock-up are a hundred and forty yards below the ford**; the hired stone store is **four hundred yards** from the ford; a guild's contract is **about four hundred yards of road**; and the only two-hundred-and-forty figure in Volume 03 is **about two hundred and forty miles of a weigh-master's own unpaid carriage** in Chapter 0109, which is miles, is another person, and is about a plate-cutter's day. **There is no two hundred and forty yards of list at a lock-up anywhere in this matter.**

## 3. Character Relationships And Consequences

**Verdict: the volume charged every principal it touched, it paid nothing back to anybody, and the three questions the audit was asked have answers that are on the page and not in the summaries. One person in this volume was wrong and was not told; two were told and did not change; one was told and changed the paper and not himself, and the volume is careful about the difference; and one person was told she was right and nobody said what it cost, and she is the one left holding a wage.**

**What the volume cost each principal, from `state/character-state.md` and the fifty chapters.** **Marek Kest, thirty-eight:** a year of his own wage on a ford, a term that ended because nobody came and about which he was not sad and had to stop and say it twice, eleven weeks of holding a bank alone out of seventeen shillings and a penny the week, his first instrument withdrawn out loud in nine sentences, a watch he cannot end, about forty-one people made findable, and about nineteen who are not on anything and are still behind a bank. **Tamsin Rook, twenty-four:** four bays four hundred and thirty miles from her own counter because a paper says so, the third line that forbids advice, the discovery that she is both the person the watch requires and the person the order compels, the not-thanking entered as a record and not a reward, and a drawer with four envelopes in it that nobody has opened in sixteen months. **Rennick Adley, thirty-eight:** went up a lane he had written down in his own book that he would not walk, and found out that the thing he wrote down in order not to do it is the only reason he can do anything at all, and put a woman who had one entry against her at two entries on purpose and does not feel good about it. **Doreth Ferrow, sixty-four:** nineteen years of chairmanship ending in a count instead of a resolution, the first name at the foot of a season written in her own hand in front of about thirty people, **right to be frightened of doing that, and saying so, and doing it**, and the sixth line that is not power and is the opposite of power. **Halla Wray, fifty-one:** four months of her own work undone by her own second idea, and about forty people now able to find where she is on any given day, which is a page and not a habit. **Tavi Venn, seventeen:** about nine hundred readings and not one hour of it paid and nobody paying her carriage, a thanks she had to ask to have withdrawn with a reason, and now a written engagement at the same rate as everybody else. **Sivra Oris:** gave away her own office's flood authority in the first month to two people without asking either, on purpose, was right, and **cannot get it back, and the size of what she can still do is one girl's reading four days a week.** **Ivet Sarn, thirty-eight:** a seventh column refused in a form with a reason and no why, about nine hundred and forty people summoned about four at a time to rooms about four hundred miles from where they live, and a line in a sixth space with no heading over it. **Leda Rell, fifty-one:** a finding against her own practice that nobody can appeal, including her, and about four hundred people she will not name. **Nell Kest, twenty:** a shop's credit gone in the same month the levy came off the mill, and one job in the town that is not hers. **Halla Wray's brother Piotr and Rennick Adley** carry a box and a lane. **Aurelia Pell, Vaunt Oris, Pell Vey, Seryn Oris, Oren Tal, Aldis Kern, Kem Ostry, Tamsin Rook, Nevin Trost, Oswin Marrow** — each owes what `state/character-state.md` records under *Owed:* and none of those entries is closed here.

**Was anything paid back to anybody? No. Nothing in this volume is paid back to anybody, and the one thing that looks like a payment is the volume's refusal of absolution.** A House lost about a hundred and forty years of fire-and-water licence on one reach and gets nothing; the only thing that came back was a **copy of the entry** enrolled four hundred and thirty miles north, and the man whose name is in the first book has not been told about the second one. Nine men in three gangs may be stood off and nobody knows yet. A family lost a shop's credit and got a levy off a mill and both of those are true. Nineteen people said no and are behind the bank. A clerk lost a year of his wage and the term stopped on the day and nobody was obliged to come. **The reader is paid — and that is a wage and not a repayment: she is paid the same rate as everybody else, on the same notice, with a term and a copy, because a favour has no notice, and she was thanked once and had to ask for the thanks to be withdrawn with a reason entered, and nobody thanked her again. The volume's one economic good is also the volume's one refusal of forgiveness, and that is the design and not an oversight.**

**Is the V3 half of the V3–V4 milestone paid, and what is unpaid? The V3 half is paid, three times, and is not to be re-paid or re-staged. What is unpaid is the reciprocity, and it is specific.** `outline/series.md` puts the milestone as *Tamsin witnesses Marek's first Burden-Bearer cost and refuses to romanticize his suffering. Their relationship becomes reciprocal and becomes romantic at the end of Volume 4.* The first sentence happened in Chapters 0138, 0148 and 0150: she put the price of his suffering in a room, refused to soften it, was not thanked, refused to be thanked, and wrote the not-thanking down as a record and not a reward. **The second sentence has not started.** What is on the page between them is a rule used six times, a court order that puts her in rooms he is in, a watch that is a **different** instrument and requires only a person not from the office, sixteen months of neither of them saying the paper is not the reason, a drawer with four envelopes, an undertaking of the fourth of the first month that is live and unexercised in the closing batch, her third line forbidding advice, and **her own finding that she is two boxes on one form.** The thing he has given her in return is the ability to be watched into rooms, which is a cost and not a gift. **That asymmetry is the unpaid half and it is the exact subject of the conversation the fourth volume owes.**

**Did any romance begin, and does anything in the pages read as intimacy, and does the volume's own lock forbid it? No romance begins and none is started, and the lock holds in every scene, and the near-misses are all refusals of intimacy rather than instances of it.** Nothing on the page reads as intimacy in the ordinary sense. What reads as intimacy is **proximity plus refusal**: she is in a bay because a paper requires it and she has said the price of his suffering three times, and a book-keeper of thirty-eight offered to enter *the paper is not the reason* in a book with no heading and **she said no** and he said he would enter his own half of it. Two things could be read as intimacy and are not. **The carriage of both of them coming out of a wage of seventeen shillings and a penny the week, nobody paying it** — that is a cost, it is named as a cost on the page, and she is not thanked for it. **Her refusal to be thanked** — that is a protection of the working relationship, not a coldness, and the volume says so in the reason she entered for withdrawing the thanks: *a person who is thanked is a person who reads for thanks.* One thing could be read as coldness and is not: **she told him he is treating his body as a place to store public cases and that the fix is not for her and she has no standing, and then she told him what she would do with the answer if he handed it to somebody, and admitted she does not know what he will do with his hands.** That is a colleague's sentence and it is the most intimate thing in the volume and it is intimacy of consequence, which is the only kind this book has.

**Is there a character who was wrong and was not told, or told and did not change? All three candidates check out and there are two more, and the volume is better than the three.**

- **A magistrate of fifty-one who entered a finding against her own practice and said nothing new about whether she was right, and refused to name about four hundred people she chose not to hold.** Chapter 0136, and behind it Chapter 0108's entry of the ninth of the second month: *I do not know whether I was right*, on the record, for nine years. She found that a good reason applied unevenly by one person in a district for nineteen years is not a rule and cannot be defended as one, and she entered it against herself, and **she added nothing about whether she was right, and nobody asked her, and the volume records that she entered it on her own motion and that it is not appealable by anybody.** She is a court and not an ally and not a villain and she has never sent a person anywhere. **Wrong and not told: no one told her and she did not need to be, which is worse.**
- **A notary of fifty-one or two who lost a hundred and forty years of his House's licence and has not been thanked and did not ask to be.** Chapter 0139 and the second half of Chapter 0140: the entry lapsed on the twenty-sixth of the sixth with his name at the foot and the only sentence in that book in a hundred and forty years that is in the first person. **He is not converted, is not a villain, does not apologise, does not explain himself again, and is not asked to do anything else. He was told and did not change, and that is by design, and the volume does not pretend it is a redemption.**
- **A man of thirty-eight who built a paper around the size of himself for eleven weeks and was told so in a room, and who then said out loud, in the same volume, that every single thing the case ever found was a person being found by a piece of paper and not one of them was a fact about the water.** Chapter 0142 she says *you have just described a man building a paper around the size of yourself*; Chapter 0148 he says the sentence about the paper. **The audit's finding: he was told and he changed the paper, and the volume is careful not to say he was corrected.** The instrument that stopped him was one he wrote himself, in the second week of the seventh month, because a man asked a woman a question in a doorway.
