The change is a change of shape and not a change of self, and the volume says so by having her refuse to be thanked and by having him not thank her and by having the last page thank nobody.
- **A fourth, and the audit's own finding: a keeper of a district water office who gave away her office's flood authority in the first month to two people without asking either, on purpose, and was right, and cannot get it back, and about nine people told her she was right, and nobody ever said what it cost.** Chapter 0133 and Chapter 0149. She said it herself in four sentences in a bay: *I have had four months to work that out after the first week of the first month and the working out took me a keeper's authority with a seal on it to get, and I would have given that back at the start of the second month if somebody had asked me.* **Nobody asked her. She is the only person in the volume who was told she was right and is left holding a wage, and there is no form in this empire for giving an authority back, and a void sheet costs four words and one minute and there is no office in that district with power to give the authority to anybody.**
- **A fifth: a chair of sixty-four who was told she was right for the first time in nineteen years and it was not said.** A foreman's letter told her the district could not have been asked, and not that she was right, and she noticed, and said so in a room, and worked out that the shape of it is the shape of the twentieth of the first month. **Nobody was wrong and nobody was at fault and a woman of sixty-four was left with the wrong shape of a sentence and put it in a book with no heading.**

## 4. Power Progression And Costs

**Verdict: every bound statement is confirmed on the page, and the volume's account of its cost is honest, and the honest account is a worse account than a man who has learned to carry things would give.**

**Confirmed, and how.** **Marek performed no reading of any instrument anywhere in Volume 03 and the only two readings in the whole manuscript remain Chapter 8 and Chapter 40.** The claim is carried on the page by four explicit sentences, and the page is the proof: Chapter 0105, *a training is a reading of a file — that is the whole of it, it is not a certification, and no certification exists*, described on the same page as human competence in a room, out of an afternoon, with no fund; Chapter 0115, *I have read no instrument tonight; I have read no instrument on any day in this matter*; Chapter 0125, *I have read no instrument and I have taken no reading*; and Chapter 0148, *no reading of any instrument was performed in the four hundred and thirty miles in either direction*. **A machine search corroborates it and is not the proof: a case-sensitive search of all fifty files for five literal phrases relating to readings returned three lines, and every one is a negation, a refusal, or the required negative usage *not a Roll Assembly*. That search is lexical and not semantic and it names no actor, so a reading described in other words or in a different case would not have been caught, and this audit says so rather than claiming more than the command supports.** **No certification was granted in any of the three volumes**, and the word is used in at least five Volume 03 chapters, always to deny one: Chapter 0105, *it is not a certification, and no certification exists*; Chapter 0107, a certification of the fourth week of the eighth month on a thing somebody else certified; Chapter 0115, *there is no certification and there is nothing in this room tonight that an auditor has done*; Chapter 0125, *there is no certification and there is no office behind this paper*; and Chapter 0148, *he holds no certification, is entered in no register of persons who read*, and *there is no certification in this matter and no register and no office and no fund*. **An earlier draft of this paragraph said the words occurred only once in Volume 03. That was a uniqueness claim no search was ever run against, it is false, and it is corrected here.** **Stage 2, Burden-Bearer, is earned and paid in Chapter 0150, in four lines on the back of a sheet, unsealed, with no number, no series and no heading and a date**, and the cost named on the face of it is *my own labour for the length of the term and my own liability for it, and there is no other cost, and nothing in this instrument is a debt of any household, and no child is named in it or is party to it.* **The counter-mark imposes no geographic tether, reads nothing, decides nothing, imposes no obligation that is not written on the paper in front of about thirty people, does not know where the ford is, flared once in the fourth hour for about nine seconds, told nobody anything, and is recorded on the page as having told nobody anything. The mark is not the price and the wage is the price.** **The three exceptions are the only three in the volume:** the word *exception* occurs **thirteen times in Volume 03, in six chapters** — 2 in 0125, 1 in 0127, 3 in 0129, 1 in 0143, 5 in 0148, 1 in 0150 — and is not used in Chapters 101–110, and **is not used of the Venn certification, which has five operative terms and is never said to have four.** The Stage 2 limit is on the face of the third instrument and is honoured: no person is entered who has not been asked in that person's own doorway, no person is entered who cannot say no, and the answer and the day are entered.

**What the volume cost him, and whether any of it got smaller. This is the audit's real business and it is answered here in a paragraph because a list would be the wrong shape for it.** In money it cost him three pounds eleven and threepence, being three weeks of seventeen shillings and a penny the week, for nineteen days of five men and a boy at forty-five pence a day, entered in a weigh-house ledger under the word *held*; about eleven more weeks of the same wage; the carriage of two people down a road four hundred and thirty miles long that nobody paid; a year of that wage on a ford at the same rate; and a sheet of good paper with his own name on it on his own desk that has not been answered. **None of it got smaller and the reason it did not is printed on the page: the wages are the price and the price is the point, and the wages were never going to shrink, because a season costs the Company four shillings a year and four shillings a year is the rent of a stone store.** In labour it cost him a year of standing in weather, about nine weeks of writing and walking, and a term that ended on the day because nobody came. In sleep it cost him nothing the volume can put a figure on and that is itself the finding, because he was not tired, he was findable; the sentence said out loud in a room of about thirty people was not about his body at all. **In findability it cost him everything, and this is the one cost that got worse instead of better:** for about eleven weeks there was exactly one name against that bank and it was his, and he could find all of ninety-one households and none of them could find him, and then he made about forty-one people findable, and a person who can be found is a person who can be asked about the bank, and **there is no version of this arrangement in which they can be found and the bank cannot** — his own sentence, in a bay, at about the ninth hour. **In other people it cost him the whole of the book's moral position, and the volume says so in a shed before any lawyer arrived and again in Chapter 0148:** his first instrument was technically good and morally cruel and put about sixty children under sixteen inside a guarantee they were not born into, and he withdrew it himself in nine sentences, and the cost of the withdrawal was that he put the bank on himself with nobody to ask, and the sentence he said out loud rather than in a room with a stove in it is that if he had not got somebody on a list of works by the fourth month of next year he had put about sixty children on a bank on his own, by himself, in a city, **and there is no form in this empire for asking them whether they would rather it was me.** So: **the volume's account of the cost is honest, and it is honest in a specific and unusual way — it declines to convert any of it into growth.** He is not better at this than he was at the fourth month of last year. He has lost the ability to audit, gained a watch he cannot end, written three instruments in fourteen months of which one is withdrawn and one lapsed for want of a person at a review, spent three pounds eleven and threepence of his own wage, given up a year of a ford, made about forty-one people findable, and told a room of about thirty people that every single thing the case ever found was a person being found by a piece of paper and not one of them was a fact about the water. **The account is not the account of a man who has learned to carry things. It is the account of a man who has learned that carrying is a wage and a term and a date at the foot of a page, and who has found out that the only thing he can move is himself and the only thing he can give away is his own labour, and who is thirty-eight and has nothing on his wrist that means anything.** That is a worse account than the other one and it is the right one, and the volume's last page says in the plainest words there are that **he is not going to stand on that bank and pretend the list is the river.**

## 5. World-Building Consistency

**Verdict: the arithmetic and the wages hold on the page, the volume's own second rule is kept in every scene where it was load-bearing and no instance of the failure mode this book is built to catch was found, and one dated phrase in Chapter 0145 is recorded below as an open repair item.**

**The arithmetic, machine-checked and read.** Chapter 0146 has it out loud in a room and it is on the page in the page's own words: *That is thirty-one and eleven and twelve, and that is fifty-four* and *So fifty-four and six is sixty* and *eighteen returns that did not come back at all, which is a different number, and the two are not added together and I am not going to let anybody add them together.* **Thirty-one plus the eleven plus the twelve is fifty-four and fifty-four plus the six is sixty, and nobody adds the six to the eighteen.** **Twenty-two seen and nine told**, and the court entered that seeing a person and telling a person are not a hearing and are not the same as each other. **The fifth heading is *Copy sold*** and Chapter 0146 says it is the only honest thing anybody ever put in that register, and Chapter 0150 repeats it. **The three thirty-ones are three figures in three places and are never put in one sentence as a pattern:** the thirty-one households behind the bank at the third-of-the-way mark, the thirty-one Mosswake households, and about thirty-one people in a lane at the north of the ford which counted itself twice in one morning and got the same number both times; with two more figures in the same family and no membership in anything — about nine hundred and forty people a magistrate made answerable, and a guild's hundred and thirty-five shillings. **Searched over the ten files of the closing batch: no sentence in Chapters 141–150 sets two of the five side by side. This audit read four of those ten chapters whole — 145, 149, 150 and 100 — and searched the other forty-six, The audit did not re-read all fifty chapters to prove a negative and does not claim to; the lock is carried in all five card files and each batch's closing section records the check against its own ten.**

**The wages, machine-checked over all fifty files and read on the page.** Forty-five pence a day on five men and a boy; three pounds eleven and threepence for nineteen days out of seventeen shillings and a penny the week, called *held*; seven shillings and sixpence is two days of that crew; one shilling and sixpence a day for a barrow of stone, a man and a boy, ninety days being a hundred and thirty-five shillings which is six pounds fifteen shillings and is more than the Company's whole money for about three years; four shillings a year, and the whole of it the rent of a hired stone store; and a reader's day between four pence and a shilling, with the finding that the reason nobody has ever paid a reader is not that the reader is not worth paying but that there is no such thing as four pence. **Every one of these is in Volume 03 and none of them moves.**

**The one rule the volume established and must then keep: a duty needs a holder that can stop.** **First, a record that is not a rule of the world: the sentence is not printed in that wording anywhere on the page.** The page's wording is a keeper of a district water office in Chapter 0102, in three parts and about nine minutes: *An office can be instructed*; *A person can stop*; *A body cannot do either of those* — a body cannot be instructed because nobody has authority over a body's conscience, and a body cannot stop because a body does not know it is doing anything, and it will go on doing it after every single person in it is dead, **and that is why a company is the right place to keep a book and the wrong place in the world to keep a duty.** The compressed form in the state files is a summary and is not a line anybody says.

**Second, was it kept in every scene where it was load-bearing, and did any character get the benefit of a duty without bearing the cost of stopping it? It was kept, and four scenes were tested because they are the four where it could fail, and one of them is the failure the volume is about.** *The keeper.* Sivra Oris disclaims her office's flood authority in the first month in writing, to do the refusing, and thereby loses the power to stop anything in her district — and she keeps the stop, which is the only part of a district flood authority anybody in that yard ever feels, and the volume says so, and the rule is kept because the thing she gave up was the power to instruct and the thing she kept was the power to stop. *The man holding it alone.* From the second of the ninth month of last year to the second of the fifth month of next year Marek Kest holds the bank by himself and bears the cost of stopping it, which is that if he stops, the water comes, and he says that sentence out loud in a room of about thirty people. Kept. *The chair.* A chair that cannot be stopped is a body, and Doreth Ferrow says so before anybody has spoken and gives herself the one thing a chair can give, which is a promise not to come back, and says that is not power and is the opposite of power and is all she has. Kept. *The guarantee.* **Here is the failure mode, and the volume states it as one: about sixty children under sixteen are inside a guarantee that costs nothing, and nobody has taken the benefit of it, and they are inside it anyway, because the instrument was offered to a body and a body cannot refuse, and the clause that disclaims household liability comes after the clause that takes the children in and is true about suing and not true about being in it.** Nobody gets the benefit of a duty here without paying; the children pay and were never asked. **That is the volume's subject and it is not a lapse in the rule, it is the rule working.**

**The rest of the bounds, machine-checked over all fifty files.** **A body in a box remained a different failure from the substituted wording and was never entered as an instance of anything:** the word *instance* occurs in Volume 03 only where the court expressly refuses the join — the north-bank works instrument of about nineteen years back, in the magistrate's own press, which the minute enters as not an instance of anything else that has been before that court and not joined to the substituted wording, **because a line between two things is a chain and a chain is an invention.** **The four appearances of *the present holder* kept their four ages and were never written as a chain:** the phrase does not occur in Volume 03 at all, so no chain could be written and no fifth instance added. **The three red things were never joined in a sentence, and are in fact not named anywhere in Volume 03** — no red thread, no red stitch, no working stitch, no sealed page. **The two sealed pages never had a third refusal,** and are not named in Volume 03. **The Ashfall bundle was not opened and is not named as a bundle.** **The counting-house mark is not converted, the year in the run is not entered, forty-four thousand eight hundred and nineteen is not read, the date the fifth term's schedule was nailed to the rail is printed nowhere in Volume 03 and its age is not restated,** and a chapter refers to that schedule without a date — twice, in Chapter 0110 and Chapter 0150, and the Batch 0005 lock permitting it once was a Batch 0005 lock and Chapters 0110 is a Batch 0001 chapter. **Vaunt Oris is not asked to do anything in the closing ten and is not named in them**; Seryn Oris is not named anywhere in Volume 03 and is credited with nothing; the counsel of the First Seat stays unnamed; the three Orises are never merged. **The guarantee is not paraphrased into a new order.** **The girl of eleven is unnamed, is given no reason for not reading, is not a device, is not a symbol and is owed nothing.**

**One open repair item, recorded and not fixed, because a repair is its own phase and this phase may not touch prose.** **Chapter 0145, line 13, prints *the first three have had headings over them since the reign of the third of the Ashfall line*.** `outline/batches/volume-03-batch-0002.md` records that this exact phrase in Chapter 0115 was corrected on the page to *about four hundred years* on the grounds that it was a canon breach, and that **a check of all ten files for Ashfall returned nothing.** **The phrase is established and legitimate in Volume 02** — Chapters 0083, 0084 and 0091 all use *the reign of the third of the Ashfall line* as a dating instrument — **so the two records conflict and this audit does not decide between them.** The finding for a repair phase is: *Chapter 0145 carries a dating phrase that the Volume 03 Batch 0002 repair established the volume does not print; the phrase is not a breach of any rule in `bible/`, changes no date, no figure and no outcome, and the bundle itself is neither named nor opened; a repair may re-date the column headings of a dyers' association's books without touching anything else in the chapter.* **The audit's judgement is that this does not bear on the fitness of the volume to hand on, and the reason is stated in the verdict so that a reader who disagrees can see exactly where.**

**The calendar, checked volume-wide and clean.** `grep -rniE "\b(january|february|march|april|may|june|july|august|september|october|november|december|monday|tuesday|wednesday|thursday|friday|saturday|sunday)\b" chapters/volume-03/*.md` over all fifty files returns **102 lines, of which 101 are the modal verb *may*, and the one that is not is `chapter-0132.md:147`, *"May I read it?"*, which is a question and not a date.** No calendar month name and no weekday name appears anywhere in Volume 03, and the closing batch's record of twelve month names and six weekday names written and corrected is a record of that batch, not a volume-wide defect. **An earlier version of this line said *about ninety lines*; the count was wrong and the substance was not, and it is 102.** This is the cheapest check in the repository and it was the one gap in this audit, and it is now run.

**And one method note, because a reader deserves to know where this audit's confidence is real and where it is borrowed.** Every claim of the form *this does not appear in Volume 03* — the substituted phrase, the three red things, the two sealed pages, the red thread — was made by a search over all fifty files and **every one of them survived contact with the search.** Two claims of the form *this is the only occurrence* were made without a search and **both were false**, and both are corrected above: the word *certification*, and the spine sentence. **So the habit to encode is: keep grepping, and treat every "the only occurrence" in this audit as unverified unless a search is quoted beside it. A claim of absence is cheap and usually right. A claim of uniqueness is the one that costs.**

**And one craft finding, which is not a canon finding and is recorded so that Volume 04 does not rediscover it. The figures in this paragraph were wrong when the audit was written and have been corrected in place; the finding itself stands, and the correction is the last section of this file, *The Review Repair Of The Volume 03 Volume-Close Handover*.** The review of Volume 03's closing batch bound emphasis at about a fifth of a chapter's lines. **Measured that way Volume 03 runs from 2% to 22% of lines, median 14%, and the bound holds: forty-four of the fifty chapters are at or under a fifth and the six over it are Chapters 0125, 0126, 0129, 0136, 0149 and 0150. Measured as a share of prose text inside `**…**`, with blockquote lines removed and the asterisks themselves removed from the denominator, it runs from 3% in Chapter 0101 to 42% in Chapter 0150, with a median of 23%, and ten of the fifty chapters sit above 30% and two above 40%.** The two chapters over 40% are **Chapter 0150 at 42% and Chapter 0140 at 41%**, and Chapter 0149 is at 39% — which is consistent with the same review's report of Chapter 0149 at 52% falling to 40% after its repair, and is a text measure and not a line measure. **On the text measure Volume 03 does not hold the bound: thirty-two of the fifty chapters are over a fifth, and that is what a Volume 04 batch has to beat.** **The bound for Volume 04 is bound on both measures, and a batch must state which of the two it measured, and with what method, before it claims the bound holds.** This is carried into `outline/volume-04.md` as a craft lock and it is craft and not a rule of the world. **Three cautions for whoever measures this again, and all three were learned at cost in this pass. One: a line count and a character count are not the same measurement, and Chapter 0140 is the proof, being 19% of its lines and 41% of its text. Two: `grep -c` counts matching *lines* while `grep -o | wc -l` counts *occurrences*, so a census built with `grep -c` is short by one for every line carrying more than one instance. Three, and this is the one that produced the false figure: a `**` opened and not closed inside a paragraph pairs, in any whole-file search, with the next paragraph's `**` and swallows the text between them, so one stray asterisk can report a chapter as more than half bold that is a tenth bold. Chapter 0117 was reported here at 59% of its text on the strength of one unclosed marker and it is 10%, and seven such markers stood in the manuscript and have been repaired. Pair asterisks inside the paragraph and never across a blank line.**

## 6. The Planned Climax And Resolution

**Verdict: both happened, and the four promises the resolution section also made are all paid, and all four are paid by saying no, and the four noes are not the same no and the audit says which is which.**

**The climax is Stage 2 and it is paid, and its size is a sheet of paper and about thirty people.** Four lines on the back of a sheet in a clerk's hand, unsealed, no number, no series, no heading, with a date. **One specified consequence** — the flood duty of the bank at the third-of-the-way mark, and of about one hundred and forty feet of ditch on the south side, and of the scour at that place, all three of which were on nobody's list — **moved from a named party, being the person who has held it, to about forty-one people in rotation, one season each, at forty-five pence a day, the same rate as every other person, with a day stated against every name, a right to give it up on one day's notice in the person's own hand into a book that has no heading, a review at the end of each term on a date stated against that name, and a reader on the same list at the same rate.** The Stage 2 limit is in the instrument's own third line and is honoured. The cost named is labour and liability and nothing else. It was read out twice in a bay in front of about thirty people, the second reading slower, **by a girl of seventeen who was not paid for the reading and is paid for the work.** **The size of the climax is that it is the smallest thing the lead has ever written that other people have to act on**, which is the inversion the whole volume exists to produce, and it is also the first time in three volumes that his power has been spent on something other than on himself: his first exception spent it on ninety-one households, his second on a ford, and this one on about forty-one people who were each asked at their own door.

**The resolution happened, and its size is about forty-one people for about ninety days, and the Company is not made a guarantor of anything.** The Slade Cut holds a public duty for a season on a list of works with a person's name at the foot of it and a date against that name. **Verified from the page and not inferred: the only instrument that made the Company a guarantor was the first exception of the twelfth of the fourth month, and it was withdrawn in nine sentences in a room in front of about thirty people, read out twice more with the second reading slower, dated the twenty-fourth of the ninth month of last year and effective the second of the fifth month of next year, so that the rotation's first season begins the same morning and there is no morning in between and no interval in which about sixty children are on a bank held by one man in a city.** The first name at the foot of the season is a carter of thirty-one who had asked to be asked last and was not, who refused to stand where a person reads a name, and who said out loud that he has not changed his mind and has changed his mind about being watched, and that those are two different things, and wanted a person in the room to write down that they are two different things. The second name is a foreman of fifty-one who has put her own name on a page for the first time in nine years and refused to state the day herself. About nineteen said no and are still behind the bank. About four people who cannot read a paragraph were never asked. A guarantee stands offered and unanswered with about sixty children inside it. **And the volume's last page is the fourth list of works in ninety-one years on that cut, on a bank and on a rail at a Reed Cut gate, on four new nails with the four old nails left in the rail and nobody pulling anything down, and it is the first one anybody on that bank will be able to point at, and a girl of eleven who cannot read a paragraph is read to by a person who stood there and is paid and can be told to stop.**

**The four promises the resolution section also made, and whether each was paid.** *The thirty-one of Mosswake gain a seventh column nobody has been able to give them, or the volume says in plain words that they have not and why.* **Paid, by saying no, in a form with a reason and no why.** *A column with a date in it and a person against the date is a hearing, and a hearing is the only thing in this empire that makes anybody answerable to anything, and this court cannot make one; a person who has not been heard has not been given a duty, and a duty given to a person who has not been heard is the thing this court has spent nineteen years declining to do in about nine hundred rooms with about four people in them on an ordinary afternoon; and there is a standing offer from a seat, open since the sixth month of last year, to pay the carriage of any person this court thinks should be heard, and it has been inoperable for about sixteen months because it pays carriage and not attendance.* And a clerk's own sentence, hers and not part of the reason: **if this court had a seventh column, about nine hundred and forty people would be summoned about four at a time to rooms about four hundred miles from where they live, and this court would be doing to them the exact thing the district of this court did to them in the sixth year, with a better motive and a worse timetable.** What they get instead is a sixth ruled space with no heading over it and one line in it, which is worth nothing at law, and the finding that a foreman four miles away arrived at the same thing nine months earlier by accident and threw a heading away twice and **nobody told her, and that is the whole of what is wrong with the last two years of my life.** *The Lowcross bill is still unpaid and nobody is liable for it.* **Paid, by saying no**, twice, in Chapter 0146 and again in Chapter 0150, with the figures: nineteen pounds three and fourpence, six pounds and eightpence committed for the quarter, about six pounds on the average of the four quarters, **no line for a bridge in that fund and there has not been one in nineteen years**, about a penny a day, four hundred and eleven on the far side, three hundred and eighty-eight crossings in the district's return for four weeks, two figures that are never the same, nobody liable, and about fifty-seven pounds of a tally gone in over nineteen years and never claimed. *What Ilyra Kest found in the Ashfall archive is still not on the page, and the road to it is a run number and a category of person who may be made a party, and that is a route and not an answer.* **Paid, by saying no, in one paragraph in Chapter 0150, as a route:** a run number in a notary's table in a city about four hundred and thirty miles up this river, a category of person who may be made a party, and a clerk of thirty-four's own note in her own hand about what a table may certify and may not — and it is a road and not an answer, and nobody has walked to the end of it and the volume did not. *A magistrate of the district court nine days out of order with a high court is still not joined, and the door to Auremar is still a door.* **Paid, by saying no, in a form, with a reason and no why**, in Chapter 0149: this court cannot hold a term in another person's paper; the two are nine days out of order; this court has not read the first and that court has not read this; **there is no form in this empire for one court to ask another court what it has found, and this court has tried to write one four times in nine years and has not sent it and has written why not against it three times**; and in a magistrate's own hand outside the form, that this court does not know, and a court that does not know should say so in a form with a day on it. **The door to Auremar is a door** and the source leaf does not travel, because a written application, a court direction and a recorded custody entry are all three required and Volume 03 supplied none of the three.

**Saying no in a form with a reason is a resolution of a kind, and all four of these are that, and they are four different kinds of no and only one of them is a refusal by a court.** Items one and four — the seventh column and the date — are **refusals by a court**: an ordinary form of that court, a box for a reason and no box of any kind for a why, the reason written in a magistrate's or a clerk's own hand and longer than the box, the day on it, a name at the foot of it, **appealable by nobody**, and each naming exactly what the court cannot do and why. Item two — Lowcross — is **a refusal by nobody**: no court was asked, no court refused, and the bill is unpaid because there is no line for a bridge in that fund and there has not been one in nineteen years, and the real cost is about a penny a day, and **that is the one of the four that no amount of asking can fix and it is the oldest of the four.** Item three — the Ashfall road — is **a refusal by the volume**: the answer exists somewhere and is not on the page, and the volume declines to advance it in one line and says so. **A form with a reason and no why is a resolution of a kind, and the two court refusals are the strongest of the four, because a court that cannot make a hearing has said so on the record with a day on it and has named the person who would have to be summoned — none — and a form that records *person to be summoned: none named* is the closest thing in this matter to a hearing anybody has been given.**

## 7. The Verdict

**The Volume 03 audit passes, and `outline/volume-04.md` was created after it and not before, in the order `PHASE_SYSTEM.md` sets out.** All six checks are satisfied on the page: the outline was delivered and exceeded in the one direction that matters; the twenty-six open threads are all recorded with a target or an em dash and none was closed, paid, forgiven, funded or quietly deleted; every principal paid and nobody was paid back; the power bound is held exactly and the volume's account of its cost is honest and is a worse account than a man who has learned to carry things would give; the arithmetic, the wages, the body rule, the four appearances of the substituted wording, the three red things, the two sealed pages and the thirty-one are all intact; and the climax and the resolution both happened with their size stated and all four of the resolution's other promises paid by saying no. **The criterion this audit used for *passes*, stated so that it can be disagreed with: a volume passes if its central question is answered on the page, if its planned climax and resolution are both on the page at the size the plan intended, if the bound obligations added to its outline after the fact are all paid, if the locks hold, and if every thread left open is recorded as open with somewhere to go. A single dating phrase in one sentence of one chapter that contradicts a recorded repair in another batch's card file, and which changes no date, no figure, no rule and no outcome, is an open repair item and not a failure, and this audit has not treated it as one and has recorded it in full so that a reader who thinks it should block can see the whole of it in one place and act on it.** The one thing this audit would not give up is the finding in section 1, that every place the page beat the plan it did so by finding the question under the outcome and handing it to a working person who is not a lawyer, and that `outline/volume-04.md` is built to expect that rather than to prevent it.

## Open Repair Items Found By This Audit — Recorded, Not Fixed

1. **Chapter 0145, the dating phrase.** *The first three have had headings over them since the reign of the third of the Ashfall line.* `outline/batches/volume-03-batch-0002.md` records that this phrase in Chapter 0115 was corrected to *about four hundred years* as a canon breach and that a check of all ten files for Ashfall returned nothing; the phrase is established and legitimate in Volume 02 in Chapters 0083, 0084 and 0091. The two records conflict. **No prose was touched. A repair phase may re-date the column headings of a dyers' association's books and change nothing else in the chapter.** Until it is repaired or the divergence is accepted, **a Volume 04 writer may not build a Volume 04 date on the Ashfall line's reign, and may not repeat the phrase as an established Volume 03 usage.**

2. **Chapter 0111 and Chapter 0130 give the south end of the hired stone store two different measurements.** Chapter 0111, line 5: *it is eleven feet by nine, and about eleven people can stand in it without touching one another.* Chapter 0130, line 3: *The south end of the hired grain store at the Slade end is about eleven feet by nineteen.* **Both describe the same room and the one with the book in it, and eleven feet by nine is the figure that matches about eleven people standing in it, so the likelier error is the nineteen — but the audit does not decide, because eleven feet by nine and eleven feet by nineteen differ by a factor of two and a repair phase has to look at which sentences depend on which figure.** No prose was touched. **A Volume 04 writer may not print either figure as settled and may not use the room's floor area in an argument.**
3. **The emphasis bound, measured two ways, and thirty-two chapters over a fifth on the text measure.** Craft, not canon. **Measured on lines, Volume 03 is 2% to 22% with a median of 14% and the bound holds: forty-four of the fifty chapters are at or under a fifth, and the six over it are Chapters 0125, 0126, 0129, 0136, 0149 and 0150. Measured on prose text inside `**…**`, with blockquote lines removed and the asterisks removed from the denominator, it is 3% to 42% with a median of 23%, ten chapters above 30%, and two above 40% — Chapter 0150 at 42% and Chapter 0140 at 41%, with Chapter 0149 at 39%.** Carried into `outline/volume-04.md` as a craft lock bound on both measures. **These figures were wrong when this item was first written, and the cause was on the page: seven unclosed `**` markers, one of them in Chapter 0117, which any whole-file search pairs across a paragraph break. All seven are repaired and the two measures now agree on all fifty chapters, and Chapter 0117 is 1,661 characters of bold in 17,174 characters of prose on fifteen lines, 9% of its lines and 10% of its text.** **A further prose repair is not proposed here and would be a separate decision: thirty-two chapters are inside a fifth on the text measure, the eighteen that are not are the ones a repair would have to look at, and the two over 40% are Chapter 0150 and Chapter 0140.**
4. **The spine sentence.** *A duty needs a holder that can stop* is the state files' compressed summary and is not a line anybody says. The page's wording is the keeper's three-part form in Chapter 0102. **No repair is needed; this is recorded so that a Volume 04 chapter does not quote a summary as if it were a quotation.**
5. **Chapter 0150 dates the reader's engagement two ways inside one chapter.** Chapter 0150, line 85 and line 163: a copy of an engagement *with a date on it of the first of the fourth month of next year*, and a sheet on the bench *with a date on it of the first of the fourth month of next year*. Chapter 0150, line 133, in Tavi Venn's own mouth: *I have been paid since the first of the fourth month of **this** year.* Chapter 0149 gives **next year** three times, in the eleventh month, which points at the same April. **The likelier reading is that line 133 is the loose one, because Chapter 0150 is in the fifth month of next year and an April already past is the same April; but the audit does not decide it, and the same chapter's *she is three weeks into it* is a second loose figure, since the first of the fourth month to the second of the fifth is nearer a month than three weeks.** No prose was touched. **A Volume 04 writer may print the engagement date as the first of the fourth month of next year, which is the figure Chapter 0149 and two of the three lines in Chapter 0150 all agree on, and may not compute an elapsed period from it.**

6. **Two figures supplied to this audit in its own instructions that are not canon:** the Lowcross fund is **nineteen years** without a line for a bridge and not twenty-nine, and there is **no two hundred and forty yards of list at a lock-up** — the landing, weigh-house and lock-up are **a hundred and forty yards below the ford**, the hired store is **four hundred yards** from it, a guild's contract is about **four hundred yards of road**, and the only two-hundred-and-forty figure in Volume 03 is **about two hundred and forty miles** of a weigh-master's own unpaid carriage in Chapter 0109. **Recorded so that neither figure enters Volume 04.**

## What A Volume 04 Writer Inherits, In One Page

**This is the first thing a Volume 04 writer reads after the state headers and it is binding. It does not contradict anything above it. Where it is shorter than the sections it draws on, that is deliberate: `state/open-threads.md` under *Rows added at the close of Volume 03, Chapter 150* and the audit's own rows carry the detail, and `state/character-state.md` carries the principals.**

**What this page is built on, and where its edges are.** It is built on the page and on five card files and on the four chapters the audit read whole — **145, 149, 150 and 100 — and on searches over all fifty files. Thirty-one of Volume 03's fifty chapters were not re-read for the audit, and anything below that depends on a sentence in one of those thirty-one is a search result and not a reading.** Every claim of absence on this page was searched and held. ****Three open repair items sit under it and none of them may be fixed by a Volume 04 batch: the dating phrase in Chapter 0145, the two measurements of the south end of the stone store in Chapters 0111 and 0130, and the two datings of the reader's engagement inside Chapter 0150. A review of this handover added a fourth, which is not a repair item and is a question nobody has answered: the building is a *hired grain store* on the page six times and a *hired stone store* four times, and a Volume 04 chapter may use either name, must use one, and may not print either as the settled name.****

**The question Volume 04 answers, and the road to it.** *What did Ilyra Kest find in the Ashfall archive that was worth dying for, and why does the original document name a blank heir.* The road is **a run number in a notary's table in Auremar, a category of person who may be made a party to an instrument that touches nobody alive, and a clerk of thirty-four's own note in her own hand about what a table may certify and may not.** **The source leaf does not travel. A written application, a court direction and a recorded custody entry are all three required, and Volume 03 supplied none of the three, and this is canon and not a plot convenience.**

**The figures a Volume 04 writer must not alter.** **31 + 11 + 12 = 54, 54 + 6 = 60**, of the fifty-four, thirty-one with a name and a door and **twenty-two seen and nine told**; **eleven** with a word and no name; **twelve** back from the post; **six copies never made**; **eighteen returns that did not come back**; **nobody adds the six to the eighteen.** The fourth column reads ***next review: none named*** thirty-one times. **The fifth heading is *Copy sold*.** About two hundred and forty copies were sold in that district and there is no list of the buyers. **The wages:** forty-five pence a day on five men and a boy; **three pounds eleven and threepence** for nineteen days out of **seventeen shillings and a penny the week**, paid in full, called *held*; **seven shillings and sixpence** is two days of that crew; **one shilling and sixpence a day**, ninety days being **a hundred and thirty-five shillings, which is six pounds fifteen shillings**; **four shillings a year**, all of it the rent of a hired stone store; **a reader's day is between four pence and a shilling**; a shilling for a question at a notary's table, fourpence for a copy, about eleven weeks for an answer, about four hundred blanks in a box under the fourth counter. **The measurements:** about four hundred and forty foot of outer face, eleven feet high, about nine acres, about thirty-one households behind it, about one hundred and forty feet of ditch on the south side, a scour at the third-of-the-way place; about nine hundred and forty names, nine hands, about sixty years, no heading, no list of persons, about four hundred dead, a fifth hand in about sixty years; the north bay thirty-one foot by nineteen holding about thirty; **and the south end of the same store is eleven feet by nine in Chapter 0111 and about eleven feet by nineteen in Chapter 0130, the two figures are not reconciled, and neither may be printed as settled.** **The counting:** about ninety-one households, about sixty children under sixteen, about forty-one asked and about forty-one yes, about nineteen no, about four never asked, out of about sixty wanted, and the third of those sums deliberately not in the book. **The distances:** about four hundred and thirty miles north and about four hundred and thirty miles down, about six days each way on a towpath and in a cart and on a bench and no chapter pretends it is shorter; **the landing, the weigh-house and the lock-up are a hundred and forty yards below the ford; the north bay is about four hundred yards from the tool shed; and the guild's contract is about four hundred yards of road from the ford to the mill wall, and nothing in Volume 03 places the hired store at a fixed distance from the ford and no chapter may.** **Lowcross: nineteen pounds three and fourpence**, six pounds and eightpence committed for the quarter, about six pounds on the average, **no line for a bridge in that fund and there has not been one in nineteen years**, about a penny a day, four hundred and eleven on the far side, three hundred and eighty-eight crossings, two figures that are never the same, nobody liable, about fifty-seven pounds of a tally never claimed. **The marks and the number:** the **counting-house mark is a mark** and is not converted; **the year in the run is not entered anywhere**; **44,819** is a number of a book nobody reads. **The Venn ruling has five operative terms** — the fourth is the new signature, the fifth is the district works, a list of works — and it is never said to have four, and **the date the fifth term's schedule was nailed to the rail is printed nowhere in Volume 04 and its age is not restated**, and a chapter may refer to it without a date.

**The dates a Volume 04 writer must not move.** The first exception is withdrawn by a document **dated the twenty-fourth of the ninth month of last year, effective the second of the fifth month of next year**; the two are one document and there is no morning in between. **The first season of the rotation began on the second of the fifth month of next year**, and a season is **about ninety days**, so **the first review falls about the first week of the eighth month of next year and is a date stated against a name.** The reader's engagement is **dated to begin on the first of the fourth month of next year**, four days a week, forty-five pence a day, with a copy, a term and the same notice as everybody else. **The second season is roughly the eighth month of next year and nobody has agreed to do it twice and nobody has been asked.** The first door-to-door asking was **the seventh of the seventh month**; **the day a chair fixed was the fifteenth of the seventh month**; **the Sorn enrolment was entered on the return of the carrier, the eighth of the seventh month**; **the factor's request for a name is dated the second of the seventh and went out on the third and has not been answered**; **Kem Ostry's refusal is dated the seventeenth of the ninth month**; **the reader's thanks were withdrawn in the third week of the ninth and the entry is of the twenty-first**; **the second sheet went up the road on the twentieth of the fifth month, four days after the window had shut, and the courier carried it nine days and worked out on the way that there was a person named on it and not the Company**; **the second exception, the ford, ended on the second of the fifth month of next year because nobody came.** **The Volume 03 batch calendar is: Batch 0001 the first week of the first month to the second week of the third; Batch 0002 the sixth of the third to the last day of the third; Batch 0003 the last day of the third to the second week of the fifth; Batch 0004 the second week of the fifth to the last week of the sixth; Batch 0005 the first week of the seventh to the second of the fifth month of next year.** **The Volume 04 batch calendar is in `outline/volume-04.md` and starts on the first week of the fifth month of next year, which is where Chapter 0150 left off, and it is not to be re-derived.**

**The people, and what each one is owed, in one line each.** **Marek Kest, thirty-eight:** no certification, no register, no office, no fund, a discharged bar, a watch he cannot end, a symptom on a wrist that reads nothing and decides nothing and does not know where the ford is, three pounds eleven and threepence of his own wage gone, a third exception on the back of a sheet findable in about four years for four pence, and a rotation he cannot act alone. **Tamsin Rook, twenty-four:** an office that does not exist with a limit that does, a third line that forbids advice, **two boxes on one form**, a drawer with four envelopes in it, one of them there since the first month of the year before last, four bays because a paper says so, and the paper is not the reason. **Rennick Adley, thirty-eight:** the book-keeper, and the sentence in the book with no heading. **Doreth Ferrow, sixty-four:** the chair, and the first name at the foot of the season, and the sixth line. **Halla Wray, fifty-one:** the foreman, second on the list, and the four months of her own work she undid. **Tavi Venn, seventeen:** the reader, on a written engagement, and not thanked and not going to start. **Sivra Oris:** the keeper, who can sign a wage and nothing else, and who gave her authority away on purpose and cannot get it back. **Ivet Sarn, thirty-eight:** the clerk, a refused seventh column and a line in a sixth space. **Leda Rell, fifty-one:** the magistrate, a finding against herself, about four hundred people she will not name, and owed Volume 04, where she testifies. **Nell Kest, twenty:** a lane, a shop, one honest sentence, a refused column, and a brother's letter still unanswered that she is still entitled to leave unanswered. **Bren Kest:** owed an honest sentence about what the work costs their family, which has been given once, in nine words, and he has not been in the volume. **Kem Ostry, thirty-one:** the carter, first on the list, not thanked, did not want to be. **Aldis Kern, forty-six:** the factor, a blank list, ninety days, six pounds fifteen shillings, nine men in three gangs, and an unanswered question. **Pell Vey:** five documents and a hand in a fourth column and a refusal to answer in the same form three times. **Vaunt Oris:** a hundred and forty years, a name at the foot of an entry, and he has not been told about the second book. **Oren Tal:** owed a scene, and **not to be made to explain himself.** **Aurelia Pell:** new to the page, his estranged daughter, a provincial archivist, and **not an ally by convenience.** **Ivet Sarn, Tamsin Rook, the clerk of thirty-four at the fourth counter, the Sarn/Hester Crane line, the girl of seventeen** — and the whole of `state/character-state.md`, which is one entry per principal and is the state at the end of Chapter 150.

**The relationship, and the lock.** **No romance has begun. The V3 half of the V3–V4 milestone is paid and is not to be re-paid. The V4 half is unpaid and may not be paid in Volume 04's first batch without a scene of its own, and `outline/series.md` puts the romance at the *end* of Volume 04.** The rule of the twelfth month of last year is used six times; a correct non-use is as good as a use. The undertaking of the fourth of the first month is live, unretired, unamended. **The paper that puts her in his rooms and the watch that requires a person not from the office in his rooms are two instruments and are never merged, and the volume has kept them apart and she has noticed.**

**The rules Volume 04 must not break, in one line each.** The two documents stay two documents and are not named; the quarterly return is in no reply; Hallis Dren's return of the eleventh of the tenth is dormant, armed and known to nobody in the story. Four appearances of the substituted wording, four ages, no chain, no fifth. Nobody wrote the substituted wording. The three red things are never joined in a sentence. The two sealed pages stay shut with two refusals between them and no third. The counting-house mark is a mark; the year in the run is not entered; 44,819 is unread; a gap in a copy is a fact about the copying; nobody is asked about the ninety-second leaf. Sivra Oris, Seryn Oris and Vaunt Oris are three people. Seryn is described as a seat and is credited with nothing she did not do. Vaunt Oris does not explain himself again. The Venn ruling has five operative terms. No certification is granted in Volume 04. The word *exception* is not used of anything that is not a Stage 2 instrument, and is not used of the Venn certification. **The substituted phrase *the present holder* does not appear in Volume 04 either, and no fifth instance of anything is added.** The guarantee is not paraphrased into a new order. The girl of eleven at the Reed Cut gate, Bel Skell, the woman of about seventy-one, the boy of fifteen and Raff Ordway are not devices, are not symbols, are not equated with each other, and are owed nothing.

**The new rules Volume 04 must keep, which Volume 03 established.** A rotation is not a list. A duty that stops is not a duty that survives stopping. A term with a review is a duty with a door in it and the door is a person. A person who is not paid is doing a favour and a favour has no notice. A reader who is paid can be told to stop. A column with a heading is a machine and a column without one is the same book with the machine taken out. A list of works has an end in it and a list of persons does not. An exception is a piece of paper and a speech can be made about people who are not in the room and a paper cannot. A body may not be made a party, may not be instructed, may not stop, and may not open its own post. A document about nobody alive cannot be asked anything by anybody either. A form with a reason and no why is a resolution of a kind.

**The spine, in the page's own words, because the summary's wording is not a line anybody says.** A keeper of a district water office, Chapter 0102: *an office can be instructed; a person can stop; a body cannot do either of those.* **A body cannot be instructed, because nobody has authority over a body's conscience, and a body cannot stop, because a body does not know it is doing anything, and it will go on doing it after every single person in it is dead. And that is why a company is the right place to keep a book and the wrong place in the world to keep a duty.**

**Craft, and not canon, and it is not a rule of the world.** Emphasis is an exception: about a fifth of a chapter's **lines** and about a fifth of a chapter's **prose text**, and a batch states which of the two it measured, and with what method, before it claims the bound holds — **Volume 03 is 2% to 22% of lines, median 14%, with forty-four of fifty chapters at or under a fifth, and it does not hold the bound on prose text, which runs 3% to 42% with a median of 23%, ten chapters above 30% and two above 40% — Chapter 0150 at 42% and Chapter 0140 at 41%, and Chapter 0149 at 39%.** `about nine` is bound to about eight per chapter and is thinned and never varied, and the figures the state files record verbatim may not be changed to thin it — **Volume 03 carries three hundred and eighty occurrences across fifty chapters, a median of 7, a range from 1 to 17, and seventeen chapters above eight.** The state files are archives, **and they do not all run the same way: `state/current.md` is newest-first, and `state/open-threads.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/continuity.md` and `state/batch-summary.md` are all append-only downward, so in four of the five a new section goes at the bottom.** **Read sections with `grep -n` and never whole files, and do not append a full restatement of what is already recorded.**

## The Review Repair Of The Volume 03 Volume-Close Handover

**A review ran on the twenty-sixth of September 2026, after the Volume 03 audit passed and after `outline/volume-04.md` and `workspace/volume-04/batch-0001/PROMPT.md` were written, and it found six things. Four are corrected in the files that carried them. One is corrected here, in prose, on the page. One is controller-owned and is recorded here and not fixed. This section is the record of all six and of every file the repair touched, and it is appended at the bottom because this file is append-only downward.**

**What was reviewed.** The volume-close phase, which wrote the Volume 03 audit into this file, `outline/volume-04.md`, and the one next phase, `workspace/volume-04/batch-0001/PROMPT.md`, and which updated the state files. The audit itself and its verdict are not reopened here: the volume still passes, and nothing in this repair touches a word, a figure, a date, a rule or an outcome of the story.

### 1. The emphasis baseline did not reproduce, and it named the wrong chapter — the cause was on the page

**The claim, in five files at once.** `outline/volume-04.md`, `workspace/volume-04/batch-0001/PROMPT.md`, `state/current.md`, `state/open-threads.md` and this file all reported that Volume 03 runs 3% to 59% of its prose text in bold with a median of 24%, that twelve chapters sit above 30% and four above 40%, and that **Chapter 0117** carries 10,172 characters of bold in 17,173 characters of prose on fifteen lines, being 9% of its lines and 59% of its text.

**Measured, the text measure does not reproduce and the lines measure does.** The lines measure reproduced exactly as stated: 2% to 22%, median 14%, on an all-lines denominator, and the bound holds. The text measure did not, and the reason is that **the measurement itself was sound and the page it measured was not.** Seven `**` markers in six chapter files were opened and never closed inside a paragraph, and a whole-file search pairs an unclosed `**` with the next paragraph's `**` and counts every character between them as emphasised. Chapter 0117's figure of 59% was produced by one unclosed marker on line 41.

**The seven sites, all of them, with what each one was.** A paragraph-level parity test over all one hundred and fifty chapter files — every paragraph and every blockquote block counted, because a marker cannot legally cross a blank line — returns exactly seven odd blocks in the whole manuscript, and all seven are listed here. **The same test over the state files finds six more, all of them pre-dating this review and none of them in a chapter: three in `state/open-threads.md` in the Volume 02 Batch 0004 rows, where a bold run opens in one cell of a table row and closes in another, and one each in `state/current.md`, `state/continuity.md` and `state/chapter-summaries.md` of the same shape. They were left alone on purpose. They are in archived rows, they are state files and not the manuscript, and this repair is not a rewrite of the archives; they are recorded here so that nobody finds them later and thinks they are new.**

| Chapter and line | What was there | What it is now |
|---|---|---|
| `volume-02/chapter-0051.md:93` | A blockquoted court form opens italic with `*` and never closes it; the line ends `in a book of his own.**` | The trailing `**` is a single `*`, which closes the blockquote's own italic. No bold was intended in that paragraph |
| `volume-03/chapter-0117.md:41` | A speech opens `"**I am not going to pretend I did it because of the seed.` and never closes | Closes after that first sentence, which is the pattern of line 39 in the same speech and of Chapter 0128's line 45 |
| `volume-03/chapter-0128.md:45` | A speech opens `"**It cannot be asked.` and never closes | Closes after that first sentence, which is the pattern of line 49 in the same speech |
| `volume-03/chapter-0142.md:69` | A speech has a balanced pair and then a third stray `**` before the closing quote | The stray pair is removed, leaving the one balanced pair, which is the pattern line 67 sets twice over in the same speech |
| `volume-03/chapter-0144.md:151` | A speech opens plain and ends `for six weeks.**` | The pair moves to the front of the sentence, making the emphatic opening line 153 uses in the same speech |
| `volume-03/chapter-0150.md:27` | A speech opens plain and ends `not about paper.**` | The pair moves to the front, making the paragraph match lines 23 and 25 of the same speech |
| `volume-03/chapter-0150.md:77` | A speech opens plain and ends `it is in the book.**` | The pair moves to the front, making the paragraph match lines 73 and 75 of the same speech |

**What was not changed.** Not one word of prose. The manuscript is **562,253 words** and Volume 03 is **176,097 words**, both unmoved, and `git diff --numstat` on the six chapter files is seven insertions and seven deletions, and every one of the seven is a run of asterisks and nothing else. No date, figure, name, rule or outcome moved, and the emphasis each of those seven passages carries on the page is the emphasis its neighbours carry, which is what the author had.

**The figures, re-measured after the repair, by both methods, over all fifty Volume 03 files.** Prose text inside `**…**` as a share of non-blockquote characters with the asterisks removed from the denominator: **minimum 3.3% in Chapter 0101, maximum 42.0% in Chapter 0150, median 23.4%; ten chapters above 30%, two above 40%, being Chapter 0150 at 42.0% and Chapter 0140 at 41.0%, with Chapter 0149 at 39.2% and Chapter 0148 at 36.9%.** Lines carrying bold, over all lines: **minimum 2.4% in Chapter 0103, maximum 21.9% in Chapter 0126, median 14.2%; forty-four of fifty chapters at or under a fifth, the six over it being Chapters 0125, 0126, 0129, 0136, 0149 and 0150.** **Chapter 0117 is 1,661 characters of bold in 17,174 characters of prose on fifteen lines: 9.3% of its lines and 9.7% of its text, and it is unremarkable, and the fifteen lines are still fifteen long speeches, which was always the true shape of the finding.** **And the check that matters for the next writer: the two methods now return the same answer on all fifty chapters, so a batch may measure either and get the figures above.**

**The craft finding survives the correction and is if anything worse.** On the lines measure Volume 03 holds its own bound. **On the text measure it does not: thirty-two of fifty chapters are over a fifth, and two are over 40%.** The bound for Volume 04 is therefore unchanged, about a fifth of the lines and about a fifth of the prose text, and a batch must still state which of the two it measured and with what method. The three cautions now recorded with the finding are: a line count is not a character count, `grep -c` counts lines while `grep -o | wc -l` counts occurrences, and **pair asterisks inside the paragraph and never across a blank line.**

### 2. A fourth divergence in one room went unrecorded — the building has two names on the page

**The finding.** The audit logged that Chapters 0111 and 0130 disagree about the size of the south end of the hired store, and it did not notice that the same room is called **two different buildings** in the same run of ten chapters. On the page: **hired grain store** six times — Chapter 0104 line 7, Chapter 0108 line 131, Chapter 0110 line 3, Chapter 0110 line 117, Chapter 0130 line 3, Chapter 0149 line 163 — and **hired stone store** four times — Chapter 0125 line 46, Chapter 0132 line 159, Chapter 0134 line 61, Chapter 0142 line 93. Both names are in finished prose in a closed volume. **Both had already reached the state files before this review and neither was ever reconciled there: ten instances of *hired grain store* and eleven of *hired stone store* across the state files, the Volume 04 outline and the five Volume 03 cards files, so a reader working from state alone would have met both and been told neither was a question.**

**What was done.** The divergence is recorded, in `outline/volume-04.md` beside the three open repair items, in the operative prompt's lock list as item four, in `state/current.md`, and as a row in `state/open-threads.md`. **No prose was touched and no name was ruled on.** `outline/volume-04.md` uses *hired stone store* throughout and now says in terms that this is the outline's word and not a ruling on the page, because the page's own count is six to four the other way and the outline is not a place to settle a question the page has not settled. **The rule handed to Volume 04: use one name and not two for the one building, never both in one chapter as though they were two buildings, and do not tell anybody which is right, because it has not been decided.**

### 3. A rule about file order was asserted for two files that do not obey it

**The finding.** `outline/volume-04.md` and the operative prompt both told the next writer that `state/open-threads.md` and `state/chapter-summaries.md` are newest-first. Measured: `state/current.md` **is** newest-first, its top section being the Volume 03 audit. `state/continuity.md` and `state/batch-summary.md` are append-only downward, as claimed. But **`state/open-threads.md` runs oldest-first downward — Volume 02 Batch 0004's rows at line 87, Volume 03's at line 176, the Volume 03 close at line 236, and the current audit rows at line 261, at the bottom — and `state/chapter-summaries.md` does the same, Volume 01 Batch 0001 at line 162 through Volume 03 Batch 0005 at line 781.** **The volume-close commit asserted the rule in the same commit that broke it.**

**What was done.** The claim is corrected in `outline/volume-04.md`, in the prompt, in `state/current.md` and in the one page above: one file is newest-first and four are append-only downward, **so a new section in four of the five state files goes at the bottom, and only in `state/current.md` does it go at the top.** The sections named as current are named. No section of any state file was moved, reordered or deleted, because these are archives and a review does not rewrite an archive's order.

### 4. The operative prompt omitted two of the three open repair items

**The finding.** `workspace/volume-04/batch-0001/PROMPT.md` carried the three open repair items only by a deferred reference to this file, so a writer working from the prompt alone could print Chapter 0145's dating phrase — which `outline/volume-04.md` forbids repeating — or either figure for the room in Chapters 0111 and 0130 as settled. **The prompt is the file a run actually holds. Anything binding that lives only behind a pointer is not binding on that run.**

**What was done.** A new bullet in the prompt's lock list names all three repair items in full, with the page's own wording for each and the consequence spelled out, and adds the fourth divergence from item 2. The same four are now in the prompt's certification bullet, which also carries the supersession below.

### 5. The supersession of the series plan was scoped to one line and the plan carries the false premise twice

**The finding.** `outline/volume-04.md` declared that the series plan's Volume 04 pressure line — that Marek's investigation threatens *his certification* — is false and superseded. `outline/series.md` says the same thing again in its **Volume 05 climax**: *Marek publicly withdraws his certification for one year and accepts joint liability for the flawed exception.* Both halves are unavailable to him, the first because he has held no certification since the twelfth of the ninth month of last year and the second because joint liability is a thing done by a person who signed an instrument, and the three he signed are all on the back of sheets in a clerk's hand.

**What was done.** `outline/volume-04.md` now declares **two** superseded lines and names the second with its chapter of the plan, and states that no Volume 04 chapter may pay either, and that what may stand in the Volume 05 climax is not decided here. `outline/series.md` was **not edited**: it is a phase-001 artefact and a review of a later phase does not rewrite an earlier plan, and the defect is in the silence about it rather than in the line, which the volume-close phase is entitled to supersede on the record. The prompt carries it as well, so the run that most needs it has it in hand.

### 6. A count was wrong, and the substantive claim held

**The finding.** The prompt and this file both said that a search of the fifty Volume 03 files for the twelve month names and the seven day names returns *about ninety lines*. The count is **102**, of which **101 are the modal verb *may*** and the single exception is **`chapter-0132.md:147`, *"May I read it?"*** — a question, not a date. The conclusion was right and the number was not, and a number handed to a writer is a measurement, so it is corrected in both places.

### 7. Two findings that are controller-owned, recorded and not fixed

**The review agent never ran as a review agent.** `--agent novel-reviewer` in `scripts/novel_runner.sh` and `.github/workflows/novels.yml` names an agent whose `.opencode/agent/novel-reviewer.md` declares `mode: subagent`, and a subagent cannot be dispatched as a primary agent, so the runner fell back to the default agent and **this review was performed under the writer's own instructions. It is therefore a self-review of a handover and its findings were checked by re-measurement rather than taken on trust; every one of the six above was reproduced from the files before anything was changed.** Both files are controller-owned and were not touched. **`PHASE_SYSTEM.md` says phase selection reads `state/phase-ledger.json`; the runner and the workflow in fact select on directory markers, the ledger is still frozen at `phase-000-bootstrap`, and nothing in `scripts/` or `.github/` reads it.** The state files' treatment of markers as the record of truth is the accurate one and this repair does not change it. The ledger was not written.

### The files this repair touched, and nothing else

**Prose, six files, seven insertions and seven deletions, all of them asterisks:** `chapters/volume-02/chapter-0051.md`, `chapters/volume-03/chapter-0117.md`, `chapter-0128.md`, `chapter-0142.md`, `chapter-0144.md`, `chapter-0150.md`. **The current phase's own output:** `outline/volume-04.md`, `workspace/volume-04/batch-0001/PROMPT.md`. **State:** this file, `state/current.md`, `state/open-threads.md`. **Not touched:** `state/phase-ledger.json`, `scripts/`, `.github/workflows/`, `.opencode/agent/`, `AGENTS.md`, `PHASE_SYSTEM.md`, `REPO_PLAN.md`, `OUTLINE_GUIDE.md`, `opencode.json`, `outline/series.md`, `bible/`, every cards file, every other chapter, and the phase markers: `workspace/volume-04/batch-0001/` still holds only `PROMPT.md` and is still the only phase the runner can select.

It changes no chapter of Volumes 01, 02 or 03, and the whole of the manuscript that precedes Chapter 151 is unmoved at 562,253 words.**
